You will be assessed on this consistency.\n    14→\n    15→---\n    16→\n    17→## Project Context\n    18→\n    19→- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n    20→- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference papers and code repository. \n    21→- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n    22→- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n    23→\n    24→## Reference Information\n    25→\n    26→- The reference **papers**:\n    27→  - \"A brain-wide map of neural activity during complex behaviour\" describes how the data were collected and is in the file `datapaper.pdf`.\n    28→  - \"Exploiting correlations across trials and behavioral sessions to improve neural decoding\" describes how the code were used and is in the file `methodpaper.pdf`.\n    29→  - A white paper about the data architecture is in `dataarchitecture.pdf\n    30→- Some parts of the papers that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n    31→- **Code** from the methods paper are in the directory `code`.\n    32→- **Data** from the data paper are in the directory `data`. Some data may be missing and may need to be downloaded. \n    33→- **Documentation** about how to access the data can be found at https://docs.internationalbrainlab.org/public_docs/public_introduction.html.\n    34→\n    35→## Decoder Task\n    36→\n    37→Temporally align based on stimulus onset\n    38→\n    39→### Decoder Inputs:\n    40→- Time since stimulus onset, continuous, time-varying\n    41→- Trial number in block, continuous, per-trial\n    42→\n    43→### Decoder Outputs\n    44→- Choice, binary, per-trial, left = 0, right = 1\n    45→- Prior probability of left, per-trial, 0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2\n    46→- Wheel speed discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n    47→- Whisker motion energy discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n    48→\n    49→## Target Data Format\n    50→\n    51→- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n    52→- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n    53→\n    54→```python\n    55→data = {\n    56→    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n    57→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    58→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n    59→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n    60→            ...\n    61→        ],\n    62→        [  # list of trials for session 2\n    63→            ...\n    64→        ],\n    65→        ...\n    66→    ],\n    67→\n    68→    'input': [  # list of sessions\n    69→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    70→            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n    71→            ...\n    72→        ],\n    73→        ...\n    74→    ],\n    75→\n    76→    'output': [  # list of sessions\n    77→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    78→            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n    79→            ...\n    80→        ],\n    81→        ...\n    82→    ],\n    83→\n    84→    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    85→    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n    86→\n    87→    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    88→    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n    89→        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    90→    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n    91→\n    92→    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    93→    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    94→    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n    95→        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n    96→        ...\n    97→    ],\n    98→\n    99→    'metadata': {\n   100→        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n   101→        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n   102→        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n   103→        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   104→        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   105→        ...\n   106→    }\n   107→}\n   108→```\n   109→\n   110→### Key Format Requirements\n   111→\n   112→- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n   113→  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n   114→  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n   115→  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n   116→\n   117→- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n   118→  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n   119→  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n   120→    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n   121→    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n   122→    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n   123→  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n   124→  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n   125→  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n   126→\n   127→- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n   128→  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n   129→  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n   130→  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n   131→  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n   132→  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n   133→\n   134→- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n   135→- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n   136→  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n   137→\n   138→- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n   139→- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n   140→\n   141→- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n   142→  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n   143→\n   144→- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n   145→  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n   146→- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n   147→  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n   148→\n   149→- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n   150→  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n   151→  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n   152→  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n   153→  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n   154→  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n   155→\n   156→## Python environment\n   157→\n   158→- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n   159→- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n   160→- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n   161→\n   162→## Decoder Reference\n   163→\n   164→- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n   165→- Optional arguments:\n   166→  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n   167→  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n   168→  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n   169→- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n   170→\n   171→### Purpose\n   172→\n   173→These functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n   174→1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   175→2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   176→3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   177→4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n   178→5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n   179→\n   180→### Consistency requirements\n   181→\n   182→Your processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n   183→- Loading of data\n   184→- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n   185→- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n   186→- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n   187→\n   188→To check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n   189→\n   190→Whenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n   191→\n   192→Discrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above. \n   193→\n   194→Carefully verify your work after every step. Experimental and behavioral variables\n   300→  - Data processing\n   301→  - Data curation\n   302→- Iterate until your understanding of the reference code, dataset, and reference texts are consistent\n   303→\n   304→**Done when**: You have resolved all discrepancies and documented your final understanding in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 4\".\n   305→\n   306→**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 5, update Step 4 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   307→\n   308→---\n   309→\n   310→### Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   311→\n   312→**Goal**: Create a detailed plan for mapping source data to target format.\n   313→\n   314→**Actions**:\n   315→- Map source data elements to target format components:\n   316→  - Which variables contain neural activity?\n   317→  - Identify ALL available experimental variables\n   318→  - Which variables should be decoder **inputs** vs **outputs**? (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   319→  - How to discretize continuous outputs (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   320→  - Which inputs/outputs should be **time-varying** vs **per-trial** (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   321→  - How does the reference code load and manipulate this data?\n   322→  - When possible, import or copy code from the reference code in `code` directory. \n   323→- Consult CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4 and reference materials to determine:\n   324→  - Temporal binning for spike rates (if applicable)\n   325→  - Temporal alignment of trials\n   326→  - Data filtering and curation rules for neurons, trials, and sessions\n   327→  - Brain regions recorded from\n   328→- Mapping should be consistent with CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4.\n   329→- Check for variables indicating valid data periods — exclude invalid data\n   330→- Note all decisions, edge cases, and data quality issues\n   331→- Plan and document **sanity and consistency checks** based on dataset size information from the reference texts, code, and data\n   332→\n   333→**Done when**:\n   334→- You have a complete mapping document in CONVERSION_NOTES.md specifying every source-to-target variable mapping under \"Step 5\"\n   335→- You have documented all decisions under \"Step 5\"\n   336→- You have documented sanity and consistency checks under \"Step 5\"\n   337→\n   338→**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 6, update Step 5 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   339→\n   340→---\n   341→\n   342→### Step 6: Script Development\n   343→\n   344→**Goal**: Write the conversion script to `convert_data.py`.\n   345→\n   346→**Actions**:\n   347→- Write all conversion code in the file `convert_data.py`\n   348→- Script should run as **`python -u convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`**\n   349→- Include options:\n   350→  - `--full` (default): Process all sessions\n   351→  - `--sample`: Process only 2 sessions for testing\n   352→  - `--show-processing`: Plot visualizations of EVERY processing step for up to 2 sessions. Save plots as `processing_<session_id>.png`.\n   353→- Reuse code from reference code bases where appropriate\n   354→- Write efficient code:\n   355→  - Vectorize loops\n   356→  - Avoid unnecessary file I/O\n   357→  - Use parallel processing if beneficial\n   358→  - Print timing information to find bottlenecks\n   359→- Plots for `--show-processing` mode should visually convince the user that every step of the conversion is correct.\n   360→  - There are no temporal misalignments\n   361→  - Discretization of continuous outputs is correct\n   362→- Write clear, well-documented functions\n   363→- Handle missing data appropriately (consult references, use sensible defaults, document)\n   364→- Validate data shapes and types at each step\n   365→- Include sanity checks (e.g., trial counts match across arrays)\n   366→\n   367→**Done when**: `convert_data.py` exists and runs efficiently without errors.\n   368→\n   369→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 7 until `convert_data.py` exists and you have updated Step 6 in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   370→\n   371→---\n   372→\n   373→### Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   374→\n   375→**Goal**: Validate the conversion on a small sample before processing the full dataset.\n   376→\n   377→**Actions**:\n   378→1. Run `python -u convert_data.py sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing 2>&1 | tee conversion_sample_out.txt`\n   379→  a. Verify the sample data structure matches the specification.\n   380→  b. Check dimensions are consistent across trials/sessions.\n   381→  c. Check that no data is missed during conversion. \n   382→  d. Validate that metadata accurately describes the data.\n   383→2. Improve efficiency of conversion.\n   384→  a. Look at and note timing information for the conversion. \n   385→  b. Estimate how long the full conversion will run. When you do this, make sure that your estimate accounts for differences in lengths/numbers of trials/sessions. \n   386→  c. If full conversion time estimate is longer than 15 minutes, speed up the code by writing more efficient code (vectorizing loops, removing unnecessary or redundant computations) and/or including parallel processing. \n   387→3. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_sample_out.txt`.\n   388→4. Analyze the output file `verification_sample_out.txt`:\n   389→  a. Verify no errors reported.\n   390→  b. Attempt to address any warnings\n   391→  c. Check input ranges and output value distributions against expectations from reference texts.\n   392→\n   393→**Done when**: \n   394→- `sample_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   395→- `verification_sample_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   396→- Processing plots show no anomalies\n   397→- Document sample statistics in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 7\"\n   398→\n   399→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 8 until `sample_data.pkl` and `verification_sample_out.txt` files exist, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 7 is filled in. \n   400→\n   401→---\n   402→\n   403→### Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n   404→\n   405→**Goal**: Verify the decoder runs successfully on sample data.\n   406→\n   407→**Actions**:\n   408→1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_sample_out.txt`\n   409→2. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n   410→3. **Check accuracy**: Verify accuracy is above chance for EVERY output\n   411→\n   412→**Investigate any issues**:\n   413→- If accuracy is low: check for conversion bugs or reconsider output representation\n   414→\n   415→**Done when**: \n   416→- Decoder training completes without errors\n   417→- Loss decreases over epochs\n   418→- Accuracies are above chance\n   419→- Document all results in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 8\"\n   420→\n   421→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 9 until `train_decoder_sample_out.txt` file exists, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 8 is filled in. \n   422→\n   423→---\n   424→\n   425→### Step 9: Full Conversion and Validation\n   426→\n   427→**Goal**: Process the complete dataset.\n   428→\n   429→**Actions**:\n   430→1. Review your time estimate from Step 7.\n   431→2. If estimated time is long (e.g. longer than 15 minutes), optimize bottlenecks first.\n   432→3. Run `python -u convert_data.py converted_data.pkl --full 2>&1 | tee conversion_full_out.txt`\n   433→4. As the code runs, update your estimates of how long processing will take. If it is much longer than your previous estimate (> 1.5x), kill the process, optimize bottlenecks, and repeat. \n   434→5. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_full_out.txt`. \n   435→6. Check that no data was lost during conversion \n   436→7. Spot-check a few sessions to verify data integrity\n   437→8. **Check for consistency** between dataset statistics in `verification_full_out.txt` and the reference texts\n   438→9. Investigate any inconsistencies, and revise the conversion script until all dataset statistics are consistent\n   439→\n   440→**Done when**:\n   441→- `converted_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   442→- `verification_full_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   443→- **ALL** dataset statistics are consistent with values from reference texts\n   444→- You have documented statistics and consistency in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 9\"\n   445→\n   446→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 10 until `converted_data.pkl` and `verification_full_out.txt` files exist and CONVERSION_NOTES.MD Step 9 is filled in.\n   447→\n   448→---\n   449→\n   450→### Step 10: Critical Review 1\n   451→\n   452→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain.\n   453→\n   454→**Check 1: Output log verification**\n   455→Read through `verification_full_out.txt` to find and fix **all errors**. Address each warning by either fixing its cause or verifying it that it is not possible to fix it. Explain why each warning could not be fixed in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   456→\n   457→**Check 2: Construct sanity checks**\n   458→- Develop at least one sanity check each on neural, input, and output data.\n   459→- These must involve loading in the original data files (NOT through your conversion code).\n   460→- Construct comparison tests and criteria using `np.allclose()`. \n   461→- These tests can be spot-checks of the data, e.g. neural activity at trial 5, timepoint 10, neuron 3. \n   462→- Fix all mismatches/sanity check failures. \n   463→- Document your sanity checks to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   464→\n   465→**Check 3: Reference code comparison**\n   466→- Compare your code to the reference code and methods. \n   467→- For each major processing step — (a) data loading, (b) neuron/trial filtering, (c) temporal alignment, (d) binning, (e) input construction, (f) output construction — identify the corresponding code in your script and in the reference, and compare the logic. \n   468→- Check whether you use the same variables, filtering, and processing as the reference code. \n   469→- Fix any mismatches. \n   470→- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   471→- Explain your reasoning for any differences in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`.\n   472→\n   473→**Check 4: Key statistics comparison**\n   474→- Verify that key statistics (number of animals, sessions, trials, neurons, input and output ranges, output distributions) match information available in the reference papers. \n   475→- Write code to investigate any discrepancies. \n   476→- Fix any discovered issues. \n   477→- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   478→\n   479→**Check 5: Check for edge cases**\n   480→- Check for off-by-one errors at the beginning/ends of trials, sessions, and subjects, or in definitions of the inputs and outputs. \n   481→- There may be minor issues in the data that your code must handle. Check that your code robustly handles issues in the original data. \n   482→\n   483→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   484→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   485→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   486→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   487→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   488→\n   489→Write a report to CONVERSION_NOTES.md describing **every** check you did, to help convince the user that the conversion code works. **Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 10\".\n   490→\n   491→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 11 until you have checked for consistency between **every** statistic in the reference texts, code, and data and documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Step 10. \n   492→\n   493→---\n   494→\n   495→### Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n   496→\n   497→**Goal**: Validate the decoder on the **complete** dataset.\n   498→\n   499→**⚠️ IMPORTANT**: This step must complete fully. Do not skip or abbreviate. Do not proceed to Step 12 until this step is completely finished.\n   500→\n   501→**Actions**:\n   502→1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --plot-samples 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_full_out.txt`. If the GPU does not have sufficient RAM for the network training, use the flag `--cpu` to specify to use the CPU to train. \n   503→2. **Wait for complete execution** — this may take significant time for large datasets\n   504→3. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n   505→4. **Check accuracy**: High accuracy is an indicator that data conversion has been done correctly. Accuracy near chance is a sign that there might be issues in temporal alignment of signals, choice of data streams, or filtering or processing of data. Compare accuracy to expectations based on the papers. \n   506→\n   507→**Done when**: \n   508→1. Full decoder training completes (the script finishes running)\n   509→2. Accuracy results are documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 11\" with a table of accuracies\n   510→\n   511→---\n   512→\n   513→### Step 12: Critical Review 2\n   514→\n   515→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain. Perform all of the following checks:\n   516→\n   517→**Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis**\n   518→- For each output, compare validation accuracy to chance (1/num_classes)\n   519→- Accuracy below chance indicates a bug, investigate and fix conversion issues to improve this. \n   520→- Accuracy below 1.5x chance may also indicate a bug, investigate whether changes in conversion logic could improve accuracy. \n   521→\n   522→**Check 2: Accuracy comparison to papers**\n   523→- Find every decoding accuracy reported in the reference papers\n   524→- Create a table comparing your accuracy to the papers's accuracy for each variable\n   525→- If your accuracy is lower than the reported accuracy, this may indicate a bug in your conversion that you must fix. \n   526→- Differences in algorithm/architecture should be relatively small because of the data dimensionality and size. Do not use this as an excuse to dismiss differences, investigate them completely before moving on. \n   527→\n   528→**Check 3: Train vs validation gap**\n   529→- If training accuracy is much higher (>1.5x) than validation accuracy for any output, investigate overfitting or data leakage. \n   530→\n   531→**If accuracy is low for an output, try these specific debugging steps:**\n   532→1. Verify the output values are correct by loading raw data and checking 3 specific trials\n   533→2. Check temporal alignment — plot neural + output for a single trial to verify they're synchronized\n   534→3. Check whether the output has enough variation (not 99% one class)\n   535→4. Check that filtering steps for neural activity streams are followed. \n   536→5. Check that processing of the data matches the reference code and papers. \n   537→\n   538→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   539→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   540→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   541→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   542→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   543→\n   544→**Report all checks and results** to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` to convince the user that the conversion and decoding has been done correctly. \n   545→\n   546→**Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 12\".\n   547→\n   548→---\n   549→\n   550→### Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\n   551→\n   552→**Goal**: Finalize documentation and organize files.\n   553→\n   554→**Actions**:\n   555→- Ensure CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete with all:\n   556→  - Key decisions and rationale\n   557→  - Checks for correctness of the conversion\n   558→  - Validation results and tables\n   559→  - Any issues found and how they were resolved\n   560→- Create a user-facing `README.md` summarizing:\n   561→  - Dataset description\n   562→  - How to load and use the converted data\n   563→  - Output format specification\n   564→  - Key statistics\n   565→- Move analysis/investigation scripts to a `cache/` folder\n   566→- Create `cache/README_CACHE.md` documenting cached files\n   567→\n   568→**Done when**: README.md exists, CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete, and directory is clean.\n   569→\n   570→---\n   571→\n   572→## CONVERSION_NOTES.md Template\n   573→\n   574→**Create this file IMMEDIATELY in Step 0. data\n   826→- **Trial alignment**: Align trials to meaningful events (stimulus onset, movement, etc.)\n   827→- **Time windows**: Choose appropriate pre/post-event windows\n   828→- **Neuron curation**: Choose filtering criteria for the quality of each neuron's recording based on reference texts\n   829→- **Trial curation**: Choose quality filtering criteria for trials based on reference texts or bad or missing data\n   830→\n   831→### Computational Efficiency\n   832→- Be mindful of memory usage with large datasets\n   833→- Use appropriate data types (float32 vs. float64)\n   834→- Consider lazy loading for very large datasets\n   835→- Implement efficient array operations (vectorization)\n   836→\n   837→## Success Criteria\n   838→\n   839→A successful conversion should:\n   840→1. Do this BEFORE exploring any code or data. Document everything in this file as you work.\n\n2. **FOLLOW STEPS IN ORDER**: Do not skip steps. Do not proceed to step N+1 until step N is complete. Each step has \"Done when\" criteria — verify them before moving on.\n\n3. **MATCH THE REFERENCE PROCESSING**: Your processing must match what's described in the reference papers and code. You will be assessed on this consistency.\n\n---\n\n## Project Context\n\n- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference papers and code repository. \n- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n\n## Reference Information\n\n- The reference **papers**:\n  - \"A brain-wide map of neural activity during complex behaviour\" describes how the data were collected and is in the file `datapaper.pdf`.\n  - \"Exploiting correlations across trials and behavioral sessions to improve neural decoding\" describes how the code were used and is in the file `methodpaper.pdf`.\n  - A white paper about the data architecture is in `dataarchitecture.pdf\n- Some parts of the papers that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n- **Code** from the methods paper are in the directory `code`.\n- **Data** from the data paper are in the directory `data`. Some data may be missing and may need to be downloaded. \n- **Documentation** about how to access the data can be found at https://docs.internationalbrainlab.org/public_docs/public_introduction.html.\n\n## Decoder Task\n\nTemporally align based on stimulus onset\n\n### Decoder Inputs:\n- Time since stimulus onset, continuous, time-varying\n- Trial number in block, continuous, per-trial\n\n### Decoder Outputs\n- Choice, binary, per-trial, left = 0, right = 1\n- Prior probability of left, per-trial, 0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2\n- Wheel speed discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n- Whisker motion energy discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n\n## Target Data Format\n\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Python environment\n\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n\n### Consistency requirements\n\nYour processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n- Loading of data\n- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n\nTo check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n\nWhenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n\nDiscrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above. \n\nCarefully verify your work after every step. 304→\n   305→\n   306→def robust_tertile_edges(values: np.ndarray) -> tuple[float, float]:\n   307→    finite = np.asarray(values, dtype=np.float32)\n   308→    finite = finite[np.isfinite(finite)]\n   309→    if finite.size == 0:\n   310→        return 0.0, 0.0\n   311→    q1, q2 = np.quantile(finite, [1 / 3, 2 / 3])\n   312→    if q2 > q1:\n   313→        return float(q1), float(q2)\n   314→\n   315→    sorted_vals = np.sort(finite)\n   316→    i1 = max(0, min(sorted_vals.size - 1, sorted_vals.size // 3))\n   317→    i2 = max(0, min(sorted_vals.size - 1, (2 * sorted_vals.size) // 3))\n   318→    q1 = float(sorted_vals[i1])\n   319→    q2 = float(sorted_vals[i2])\n   320→    if q2 <= q1:\n   321→        larger = sorted_vals[sorted_vals > q1]\n   322→        q2 = float(larger[0]) if larger.size else q1\n   323→    return q1, q2\n   324→\n   325→\n   326→def digitize_three_bins(values: np.ndarray, edges: tuple[float, float]) -> np.ndarray:\n   327→    low, high = edges\n   328→    if high <= low:\n   329→        return np.zeros(values.shape, dtype=np.int8)\n   330→    return np.digitize(values, bins=np.array([low, high], dtype=np.float32), right=False).astype(np.int8)\n   331→\n   332→\n   333→def load_good_spikes_and_regions(spec: SessionSpec, brain_regions: BrainRegions) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n   334→    spike_times_all: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   335→    spike_clusters_all: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   336→    cluster_regions_all: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   337→    cluster_offset = 0\n   338→\n   339→    for probe_name in spec.probe_names:\n   340→        probe_dir = spec.session_path / \"alf\" / probe_name / \"pykilosort\"\n   341→        metrics_path = resolve_latest(probe_dir, \"**/clusters.metrics.pqt\")\n   342→        clusters_channels_path = resolve_latest(probe_dir, \"**/clusters.channels.npy\")\n   343→        channels_region_ids_path = resolve_latest(probe_dir, \"**/channels.brainLocationIds_ccf_2017.npy\")\n   344→        spikes_times_path = resolve_latest(probe_dir, \"**/spikes.times.npy\")\n   345→        spikes_clusters_path = resolve_latest(probe_dir, \"**/spikes.clusters.npy\")\n   346→\n   347→        metrics = pd.read_parquet(metrics_path, columns=[\"label\"])\n   348→        good_rows = metrics[\"label\"].to_numpy(copy=False) >= 1\n   349→        if not np.any(good_rows):\n   350→            continue\n   351→\n   352→        spikes_times = np.load(spikes_times_path, mmap_mode=\"r\")\n   353→        spikes_clusters = np.load(spikes_clusters_path, mmap_mode=\"r\")\n   354→        n_clusters = good_rows.shape[0]\n   355→        valid_spikes = spikes_clusters < n_clusters\n   356→        if np.all(valid_spikes):\n   357→            spike_mask = good_rows[spikes_clusters]\n   358→        else:\n   359→            spike_mask = np.zeros(spikes_clusters.shape[0], dtype=bool)\n   360→            spike_mask[valid_spikes] = good_rows[spikes_clusters[valid_spikes]]\n   361→        remap = np.full(n_clusters, -1, dtype=np.int32)\n   362→        remap[good_rows] = np.arange(int(good_rows.sum()), dtype=np.int32)\n   363→        good_spike_times = np.asarray(spikes_times[spike_mask], dtype=np.float64)\n   364→        good_spike_clusters = remap[spikes_clusters[spike_mask]] + cluster_offset\n   365→\n   366→        cluster_channels = np.load(clusters_channels_path, mmap_mode=\"r\")[good_rows]\n   367→        channel_region_ids = np.load(channels_region_ids_path, mmap_mode=\"r\")\n   368→        region_ids = np.zeros(cluster_channels.shape[0], dtype=np.int64)\n   369→        valid_channel = (cluster_channels >= 0) & (cluster_channels < channel_region_ids.shape[0])\n   370→        region_ids[valid_channel] = channel_region_ids[cluster_channels[valid_channel]]\n   371→        cluster_regions = brain_regions.id2acronym(region_ids, mapping=\"Beryl\").astype(str)\n   372→\n   373→        spike_times_all.append(good_spike_times)\n   374→        spike_clusters_all.append(good_spike_clusters)\n   375→        cluster_regions_all.append(cluster_regions)\n   376→        cluster_offset += cluster_regions.shape[0]\n   377→\n   378→    if cluster_offset == 0:\n   379→        raise ValueError(f\"No good units remained for {spec.eid}\")\n   380→\n   381→    merged_spike_times = np.concatenate(spike_times_all)\n   382→    merged_spike_clusters = np.concatenate(spike_clusters_all)\n   383→    order = np.argsort(merged_spike_times)\n   384→    merged_spike_times = merged_spike_times[order]\n   385→    merged_spike_clusters = merged_spike_clusters[order]\n   386→    merged_cluster_regions = np.concatenate(cluster_regions_all)\n   387→    return merged_spike_times, merged_spike_clusters, merged_cluster_regions\n   388→\n   389→\n   390→def bin_spikes_by_trial(\n   391→    spike_times: np.ndarray,\n   392→    spike_clusters: np.ndarray,\n   393→    align_times: np.ndarray,\n   394→    n_units: int,\n   395→    binsize: float = BINSIZE,\n   396→    time_window: tuple[float, float] = TIME_WINDOW,\n   397→) -> list[np.ndarray]:\n   398→    interval_begs = align_times + time_window[0]\n   399→    interval_ends = align_times + time_window[1]\n   400→    n_bins = int(np.ceil((time_window[1] - time_window[0]) / binsize))\n   401→    start_idx = np.searchsorted(spike_times, interval_begs, side=\"left\")\n   402→    end_idx = np.searchsorted(spike_times, interval_ends, side=\"left\")\n   403→\n   404→    out: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   405→    for i in range(len(align_times)):\n   406→        i0 = start_idx[i]\n   407→        i1 = end_idx[i]\n   408→        if i1 <= i0:\n   409→            out.append(np.zeros((n_units, n_bins), dtype=np.float16))\n   410→            continue\n   411→\n   412→        rel = spike_times[i0:i1] - interval_begs[i]\n   413→        bin_idx = np.floor(rel / binsize).astype(np.int64)\n   414→        valid = (bin_idx >= 0) & (bin_idx < n_bins)\n   415→        if not np.any(valid):\n   416→            out.append(np.zeros((n_units, n_bins), dtype=np.float16))\n   417→            continue\n   418→\n   419→        flat = spike_clusters[i0:i1][valid] * n_bins + bin_idx[valid]\n   420→        counts = np.bincount(flat, minlength=n_units * n_bins).reshape(n_units, n_bins)\n   421→        out.append(counts.astype(np.float16))\n   422→    return out\n   423→\n   424→\n   425→def map_choice(raw_choice: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n   426→    mapped = np.empty(raw_choice.shape[0], dtype=np.int8)\n   427→    mapped[raw_choice == 1] = 0\n   428→    mapped[raw_choice == -1] = 1\n   429→    if not np.all(np.isin(raw_choice, [-1, 1])):\n   430→        raise ValueError(\"Unexpected choice values encountered after filtering.\")\n   431→    return mapped\n   432→\n   433→\n   434→def map_prior(raw_prior: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n   435→    out = np.empty(raw_prior.shape[0], dtype=np.int8)\n   436→    mapper = {0.2: 0, 0.5: 1, 0.8: 2}\n   437→    for i, val in enumerate(raw_prior):\n   438→        key = round(float(val), 1)\n   439→        if key not in mapper:\n   440→            raise ValueError(f\"Unexpected probabilityLeft value {val}\")\n   441→        out[i] = mapper[key]\n   442→    return out\n   443→\n   444→\n   445→def build_session_payload(\n   446→    prepared: PreparedSession,\n   447→    wheel_edges: tuple[float, float],\n   448→    whisker_edges: tuple[float, float],\n   449→) -> tuple[str, list[np.ndarray], list[np.ndarray], list[np.ndarray], np.ndarray, float]:\n   450→    session_start = time.time()\n   451→    brain_regions = BrainRegions()\n   452→    kept_align_times = prepared.trials.loc[prepared.keep_mask, ALIGN_EVENT].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)\n   453→    spike_times, spike_clusters, cluster_regions = load_good_spikes_and_regions(prepared.spec, brain_regions)\n   454→    neural_trials = bin_spikes_by_trial(\n   455→        spike_times=spike_times,\n   456→        spike_clusters=spike_clusters,\n   457→        align_times=kept_align_times,\n   458→        n_units=cluster_regions.shape[0],\n   459→    )\n   460→\n   461→    choice = map_choice(prepared.choice_raw)\n   462→    prior = map_prior(prepared.prior_raw)\n   463→    wheel_bins = digitize_three_bins(prepared.wheel_cont, wheel_edges)\n   464→    whisker_bins = digitize_three_bins(prepared.whisker_cont, whisker_edges)\n   465→\n   466→    session_input: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   467→    session_output: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   468→    for trial_idx in range(len(neural_trials)):\n   469→        inp = np.vstack(\n   470→            [\n   471→                COMMON_RELATIVE_TIMES,\n   472→                np.full(NBINS, prepared.trial_number_in_block[trial_idx], dtype=np.float32),\n   473→            ]\n   474→        ).astype(np.float32)\n   475→        out = np.vstack(\n   476→            [\n   477→                np.full(NBINS, choice[trial_idx], dtype=np.int8),\n   478→                np.full(NBINS, prior[trial_idx], dtype=np.int8),\n   479→                wheel_bins[trial_idx],\n   480→                whisker_bins[trial_idx],\n   481→            ]\n   482→        ).astype(np.int8)\n   483→        session_input.append(inp)\n   484→        session_output.append(out)\n   485→\n   486→    elapsed = time.time() - session_start\n   487→    return prepared.spec.eid, neural_trials, session_input, session_output, cluster_regions.astype(str), elapsed\n   488→\n   489→\n   490→def make_processing_plot(\n   491→    prepared: PreparedSession,\n   492→    neural_trials: list[np.ndarray],\n   493→    wheel_edges: tuple[float, float],\n   494→    whisker_edges: tuple[float, float],\n   495→) -> None:\n   496→    trial_idx = 0\n   497→    wheel_bins = digitize_three_bins(prepared.wheel_cont[trial_idx], wheel_edges)\n   498→    whisker_bins = 526→    axes[3].axis(\"off\")\n   527→    axes[3].text(\n   528→        0.01,\n   529→        0.95,\n   530→        \"\\n\".join(\n   531→            [\n   532→                f\"Kept trials:              f\"Alignment window: {TIME_WINDOW} s, binsize={BINSIZE:.3f} s\",\n   538→            ]\n   539→        ),\n   540→        va=\"top\",\n   541→        ha=\"left\",\n   542→        fontsize=12,\n   543→        family=\"monospace\",\n   544→    )\n   545→\n   546→    fig.tight_layout()\n   547→    fig.savefig(f\"processing_{prepared.spec.eid}.png\", dpi=150)\n   548→    plt.close(fig)\n   549→\n   550→\n   551→def build_dataset(\n   552→    prepared_sessions: list[PreparedSession],\n   553→    wheel_edges: tuple[float, float],\n   554→    whisker_edges: tuple[float, float],\n   555→    show_processing: bool,\n   556→    n_workers: int,\n   557→) -> dict[str, Any]:\n   558→    neural_all: list[list[np.ndarray]] = []\n   559→    input_all: list[list[np.ndarray]] = []\n   560→    output_all: list[list[np.ndarray]] = []\n   561→    subject_names = sorted({ps.spec.subject for ps in prepared_sessions})\n   562→    subject_to_idx = {subject: i for i, subject in enumerate(subject_names)}\n   563→    subject_idx_list: list[int] = []\n   564→\n   565→    all_region_names: set[str] = set()\n   566→    session_region_names: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   567→    session_ids: list[str] = []\n   568→    excluded_session_notes: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n   569→\n   570→    t0 = time.time()\n   571→    if n_workers == 1:\n   572→        for session_i, prepared in enumerate(prepared_sessions, start=1):\n   573→            eid, neural_trials, session_input, session_output, cluster_regions, elapsed = build_session_payload(\n   574→                prepared, wheel_edges, whisker_edges\n   575→            )\n   576→            neural_all.append(neural_trials)\n   577→            input_all.append(session_input)\n   578→            output_all.append(session_output)\n   579→            session_region_names.append(cluster_regions)\n   580→            all_region_names.update(cluster_regions.tolist())\n   581→            session_ids.append(eid)\n   582→            subject_idx_list.append(subject_to_idx[prepared.spec.subject])\n   583→\n   584→            print(\n   585→                f\"[build] {session_i:03d}/{len(prepared_sessions)} {eid} \"\n   586→                f\"trials={len(neural_trials)} neurons={cluster_regions.shape[0]} \"\n   587→                f\"time={elapsed:.2f}s\"\n   588→            )\n   589→\n   590→            if show_processing and session_i <= 2:\n   591→                make_processing_plot(prepared, neural_trials, wheel_edges, whisker_edges)\n   592→    else:\n   593→        with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=n_workers) as pool:\n   594→            future_to_index = {\n   595→                pool.submit(build_session_payload, prepared, wheel_edges, whisker_edges): idx\n   596→                for idx, prepared in enumerate(prepared_sessions)\n   597→            }\n   598→            for completed_i, future in enumerate(as_completed(future_to_index), start=1):\n   599→                idx = future_to_index[future]\n   600→                prepared = prepared_sessions[idx]\n   601→                eid, neural_trials, session_input, session_output, cluster_regions, elapsed = future.result()\n   602→\n   603→                neural_all.append(neural_trials)\n   604→                input_all.append(session_input)\n   605→                output_all.append(session_output)\n   606→                session_region_names.append(cluster_regions)\n   607→                all_region_names.update(cluster_regions.tolist())\n   608→                session_ids.append(eid)\n   609→                subject_idx_list.append(subject_to_idx[prepared.spec.subject])\n   610→\n   611→                print(\n   612→                    f\"[build] {completed_i:03d}/{len(prepared_sessions)} {eid} \"\n   613→                    f\"trials={len(neural_trials)} neurons={cluster_regions.shape[0]} \"\n   614→                    f\"time={elapsed:.2f}s\"\n   615→                )\n   616→\n   617→    brain_region_list = sorted(all_region_names)\n   618→    brain_region_to_idx = {name: i for i, name in enumerate(brain_region_list)}\n   619→    brain_region_idx = [\n   620→        np.array([brain_region_to_idx[name] for name in session_regions], dtype=np.int64)\n   621→        for session_regions in session_region_names\n   622→    ]\n   623→\n   624→    print(f\"[build] completed in {time.time() - t0:.2f}s\")\n   625→\n   626→    data = {\n   627→        \"neural\": neural_all,\n   628→        \"input\": input_all,\n   629→        \"output\": output_all,\n   630→        \"subjects\": subject_names,\n   631→        \"subject_idx\": np.array(subject_idx_list, dtype=np.int64),\n   632→        \"brain_regions\": brain_region_list,\n   633→        \"brain_region_idx\": brain_region_idx,\n   634→        \"input_names\": INPUT_NAMES,\n   635→        \"output_names\": OUTPUT_NAMES,\n   636→        \"output_values\": OUTPUT_VALUES,\n   637→        \"metadata\": {\n   638→            \"task_description\": (\n   639→                \"Stimulus-onset-aligned IBL brain-wide map decoding task with well-isolated ephys units; \"\n   640→                \"decode choice, block prior, wheel speed bin, and whisker motion energy bin.\"\n   641→            ),\n   642→            \"time_bin_size\": 20.0,\n   643→            \"temporal_alignment_event\": \"stimulus onset (stimOn_times)\",\n   644→            \"off_start\": TIME_WINDOW[0],\n   645→            \"off_end\": TIME_WINDOW[1],\n   646→            \"session_ids\": session_ids,\n   647→            \"source_release\": \"code/code_zhang2025/data/bwm_release.csv\",\n   648→            \"neuron_qc_rule\": \"clusters.metrics.label >= 1\",\n   649→            \"trial_filtering\": {\n   650→                \"required_non_nan\": [\n   651→                    \"stimOn_times\",\n   652→                    \"choice\",\n   653→                    \"feedback_times\",\n   654→                    \"probabilityLeft\",\n   655→                    \"firstMovement_times\",\n   656→                    \"feedbackType\",\n   657→                ],\n   658→                \"reaction_time_s\": [0.08, 2.0],\n   659→                \"max_trial_len_s\": 10.0,\n   660→                \"exclude_nochoice\": True,\n   661→            },\n   662→            \"dynamic_output_bin_edges\": {\n   663→                \"wheel_speed_bin\": [float(wheel_edges[0]), float(wheel_edges[1])],\n   664→                \"whisker_motion_energy_bin\": [float(whisker_edges[0]), float(whisker_edges[1])],\n   665→            },\n   666→            \"dynamic_output_binning\": \"global tertile bins over all kept timepoints\",\n   667→            \"common_timepoints_relative_to_alignment_s\": COMMON_RELATIVE_TIMES.astype(np.float32),\n   668→            \"excluded_session_notes\": excluded_session_notes,\n   669→            \"n_workers\": n_workers,\n   670→        },\n   671→    }\n   672→    return data\n   673→\n   674→\n   675→def summarize_prepared(prepared_sessions: list[PreparedSession]) -> None:\n   676→    n_trials = np.array([ps.keep_mask.sum() for ps in prepared_sessions], dtype=np.int64)\n   677→    n_neurons = np.array([ps.n_good_units for ps in prepared_sessions], dtype=np.int64)\n   678→    print(\n   679→        \"[summary] sessions={} subjects={} trials_total={} trials_mean={:.2f} neurons_total={} neurons_mean={:.2f}\".format(\n   680→            len(prepared_sessions),\n   681→            len({ps.spec.subject for ps in prepared_sessions}),\n   682→            int(n_trials.sum()),\n   683→            float(n_trials.mean()) if n_trials.size else 0.0,\n   684→            int(n_neurons.sum()),\n   685→            float(n_neurons.mean()) if n_neurons.size else 0.0,\n   686→        )\n   687→    )\n   688→\n   689→\n   690→def main() -> None:\n   691→    args = parse_args()\n   692→    mode = \"sample\" if args.sample else \"full\"\n   693→    n_workers = choose_n_workers(mode, args.show_processing)\n   694→\n   695→    sessions = load_release_sessions()\n   696→    print(f\"[setup] loaded {len(sessions)} release sessions from {RELEASE_CSV}\")\n   697→    print(f\"[setup] using {n_workers} worker(s)\")\n   698→\n   699→    if args.sample:\n   700→        target_sessions = sessions\n   701→    else:\n   702→        target_sessions = sessions\n   703→\n   704→    prepared_sessions: list[PreparedSession] = []\n   705→    excluded: list[tuple[str, str]] = []\n   706→\n   707→    first_pass_start = time.time()\n   708→    if n_workers == 1:\n   709→        results_iter = (build_session_behavior_safe(spec) for spec in target_sessions)\n   710→    else:\n   711→        with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=n_workers) as pool:\n   712→            results_iter = pool.map(build_session_behavior_safe, target_sessions)\n   713→\n   714→    for idx, (eid, prepared, error) in enumerate(results_iter, start=1):\n   715→        if prepared is None:\n   716→            excluded.append((eid, error or \"unknown\"))\n   717→        else:\n   718→            prepared_sessions.append(prepared)\n   719→            print(\n   720→                f\"[pass1] {idx:03d}/{len(target_sessions)} {eid} \"\n   721→                f\"kept_trials={prepared.keep_mask.sum()} good_units={prepared.n_good_units} \"\n   722→                f\"whisker={prepared.whisker_source}\"\n   723→            )\n   724→            if args.sample and len(prepared_sessions) >= 2:\n   725→                break\n   726→\n   727→    if not prepared_sessions:\n   728→        raise RuntimeError(\"No sessions passed preprocessing.\")\n   729→\n   730→    print(f\"[pass1] completed in {time.time() - first_pass_start:.2f}s\")\n   731→    print(f\"[pass1] excluded sessions: {len(excluded)}\")\n   732→    summarize_prepared(prepared_sessions)\n   733→\n   734→    all_wheel = np.concatenate([ps.wheel_cont.reshape(-1) for ps in prepared_sessions]).astype(np.float32)\n   735→    all_whisker = np.concatenate([ps.whisker_cont.reshape(-1) for ps in prepared_sessions]).astype(np.float32)\n   736→    wheel_edges = robust_tertile_edges(all_wheel)\n   737→    whisker_edges = robust_tertile_edges(all_whisker)\n   738→    print(f\"[bins] wheel tertile edges={wheel_edges}\")\n   739→    print(f\"[bins] whisker tertile edges={whisker_edges}\")\n   740→\n   741→    data = build_dataset(\n   742→        prepared_sessions=prepared_sessions,\n   743→        wheel_edges=wheel_edges,\n   744→        whisker_edges=whisker_edges,\n   745→        show_processing=args.show_processing,\n   746→        n_workers=n_workers,\n   747→    )\n   748→ time_window: tuple[float, float] = TIME_WINDOW,\n) -> list[np.ndarray]:\n    interval_begs = align_times + time_window[0]\n    interval_ends = align_times + time_window[1]\n    n_bins = np.ndarray:\n    out = np.empty(raw_prior.shape[0], dtype=np.int8)\n    mapper = {0.2: 0, 0.5: 1, 0.8: 2}\n    for i, val in enumerate(raw_prior):\n        key = round(float(val), 1)\n        if key causal window of W = 10 bins for regression\n   173→  - binary variables evaluated with balanced accuracy\n   174→  - continuous variables evaluated with R2\n   175→- Motion energy definition from data paper:\n   176→  - mean absolute pixel difference between adjacent frames in a whisker-pad bounding box anchored between nose tip and eye\n   177→- Data architecture white paper:\n   178→  - raw data are stored in ALF / ONE-style dataset types\n   179→  - session-relative files and revisions are expected; this matches the versioned `alf/#...#/` files observed locally\n   180→\n   181→### Curation Steps\n   182→\n   183→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   184→- Public release kept 621,733 units including multineuron activity.\n   185→- Main data-paper analyses further defined “well-isolated neurons” using RIGOR single-unit QC:\n   186→  - amplitude > 50 uV\n   187→  - noise cut-off < 20 uV\n   188→  - refractory-period violation criterion\n   189→- Final main analyses further restricted to grey-matter regions with at least 5 well-isolated neurons per session and at least 2 such sessions.\n   190→\n   191→**Trial curation rules**:\n   192→- Release / analysis trial exclusions in data paper:\n   193→  - exclude trials missing `choice`, `probabilityLeft`, `feedbackType`, `feedback_times`, `stimOn_times`, or `firstMovement_times`\n   194→  - exclude trials with reaction time (`firstMovement_times - stimOn_times`) outside 0.08–2.00 s\n   195→- Sessions in public release had to satisfy:\n   196→  - at least 250 trials\n   197→  - at least 90% correct on 100% contrast trials for both left and right blocks\n   198→  - at least 3 incorrect-choice trials after exclusions\n   199→  - hardware QC threshold\n   200→- Main paper also notes only sessions with at least 400 trials were retained for further analyses.\n   201→\n   202→### Decoders Trained\n   203→| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n   204→| Choice | multi-session behavioral-state model improves over baseline; example figure shows choice AUC improving from about 0.66 baseline to about 0.79 on an example session |\n   205→| Prior | LG-AR1 improves over baseline; example figure shows prior correlation improving from about 0.05 baseline to about 0.34 multi-session and about 0.65 oracle on an example session |\n   206→| Wheel speed | paper evaluates with R2; figures indicate multi-session RRR outperforms baseline / single-session models |\n   207→| Whisker motion energy | paper evaluates with R2; figures indicate multi-session RRR outperforms baseline / single-session models |\n   208→\n   209→---\n   210→\n   211→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   212→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   213→\n   214→### Discrepancies Found\n   215→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n   216→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   217→| Session universe | `code/code_zhang2025/data/bwm_release.csv` contains 699 probe insertions / 459 unique sessions / 139 subjects; `repro_ephys_release.txt` is only a 40-session example subset | local raw session folders: 461 sessions / 141 subjects; `Brainwidemap/sessions.pqt`: 480 sessions / 143 subjects; 459-session / 699-insertion BWM release as authoritative for conversion because it matches both `bwm_release.csv` and the data paper release counts. Ignore extra/newer sessions in local registry snapshots unless needed only for file lookup. |\n   218→| Probe counts | code freeze implies 219 one-probe and 240 two-probe sessions | local folders show 221 one-probe and 240 two-probe session directories | data paper: “most recordings using 2 simultaneous probe insertions” and total 699 insertions | Use `bwm_release.csv` / probe metadata to define valid insertions for the target release; local folders appear to include extra sessions outside the frozen release. |\n   219→| Method-paper session count | code examples do not mention 433 explicitly | local cache has enough sessions to support a large subset but not an exact 433 count from folders alone | method paper: 433 IBL sessions across 270 brain regions | Interpret 433 as a downstream analysis subset of the 459-session release after additional modality / analysis availability constraints. Do not use 433 as the raw starting session count. |\n   220→| Neuron QC policy | `load_spiking_data(..., qc=None)` loads all clusters; metadata stores `good_clusters = label >= 1` but caching does not filter on it | cluster metrics files contain `label`, `firing_rate`, `noise_cutoff`, etc.; aggregating the frozen 699 insertions gives 621,733 raw units and exactly 75,708 units with `label >= 1` | data paper main analyses emphasize 75,708 well-isolated neurons; method paper text says “using all neurons, sorted by Kilosort 2.5, from each session” | Use `label >= 1` units for the converted decoder dataset. This exactly reproduces the paper’s well-isolated-neuron count and keeps the dense per-trial format computationally feasible, while still deriving the mask from the same cluster metrics bundled with the reference release. Preserve raw QC metadata in `metadata`. |\n   221→| Trial filtering | code excludes missing `stimOn_times`, `choice`, `feedback_times`, `probabilityLeft`, `firstMovement_times`, `feedbackType`; RT outside 0.08–2.0 s; no-choice trials; plus `max_trial_len=10.0` | trial tables contain all required columns and some no-choice / NaN rows | data paper excludes the same missing events and RT 0.08–2.00 s; release sessions need >=250 trials; main analyses often >=400 trials | Mandatory filters are the shared missing-event and RT filters. Keep `exclude_nochoice=True` and `max_trial_len=10.0` to remain consistent with the provided code path, while noting these are stricter than the minimal text summary. |\n   222→| Temporal alignment for `choice` | code cache aligns to `stimOn_times` with window `(-0.5, 1.5)` and 20 ms bins | raw data contain `stimOn_times` and `firstMovement_times` | method paper also aligns choice to stimulus onset | No discrepancy. Use stimulus-onset alignment for choice. |\n   223→| Temporal alignment for `prior` | provided code cache does not special-case prior and would align to `stimOn_times` with the same 2 s / 20 ms window as other targets | raw data contain `probabilityLeft` per trial | method paper says prior uses stimulus onset but a different window (`-0.6` to `-0.1` s) and 50 ms bins | For this task, decoder inputs/outputs must share a common stimulus-onset-aligned trial grid. Use a common 20 ms stimulus-onset grid for the converted dataset, and document that this is a task-imposed simplification relative to the method paper’s prior-specific window. |\n   224→| Temporal alignment for `wheel-speed` and `whisker-motion-energy` | provided code cache aligns all behaviors to `stimOn_times` | raw data provide continuous wheel and whisker streams plus `stimOn_times` and `firstMovement_times` | method paper says dynamic behaviors should align to `firstMovement_times` and run to +1 s | User explicitly requires “Temporally align based on stimulus onset.” Therefore, use stimulus-onset alignment for dynamic outputs as a deliberate task override, while keeping the reference code’s binning, interpolation style, and trial masking wherever possible. |\n   225→| Binning of dynamic behavior | code uses 20 ms bins and linear interpolation inside each interval | raw wheel / motion-energy streams have irregular session-wide sampling (wheel) or camera-rate sampling (60 Hz / 150 Hz) | method paper dynamic behavior bins are 20 ms | No substantive discrepancy. Use 20 ms bins with trial-wise interpolation. |\n   226→| Binning of prior | code examples / cache infrastructure assume 20 ms grids; prior notebooks treat prior as scalar over trials | `probabilityLeft` is available per trial; no continuous prior trace in raw data | method paper prior bins neural data at 50 ms and decodes a scalar per trial | Represent prior as per-trial categorical output on the common trial grid in the converted dataset, duplicating across time if needed for shape consistency only if required later. |\n   227→| Motion-energy source | code prefers left whisker motion energy and falls back to right camera if missing | 434 local sessions have left motion energy; 421 have right; 441 have at least one side | data paper computes whisker-pad motion energy from left and right videos | Prefer left camera when available, else right, matching the provided code and the local coverage pattern. |\n   228→| Data organization | code assumes ONE / ALF loading with revisions and dataset types | local files are indeed stored under revisioned `alf/#...#/` paths and `probeXX/pykilosort/...` | data architecture paper describes ONE / ALF dataset-type conventions | Follow ALF / ONE naming and revision handling already present in local cache. |\n   229→\n   230→Final understanding:\n   231→- Start from the frozen 459-session / 699-insertion BWM release, not from all locally visible session folders.\n   232→- Apply the shared trial-quality filters from the papers plus the provided code’s `exclude_nochoice=True` and `max_trial_len=10.0`.\n   233→- Use `label >= 1` well-isolated units because this reproduces the paper’s 75,708-neuron statistic exactly and is the release-consistent dense representation that remains computationally tractable for the provided decoder.\n   234→- Use a common 20 ms stimulus-onset-aligned grid for the converted dataset because the user explicitly requires stimulus-onset alignment and the target decoder needs common trial shapes across neural, input, and output streams.\n   235→- Treat method-paper target-specific alignment differences (especially dynamic behaviors and prior) as important reference context, but as overridden where they conflict with the user’s explicit decoder specification.\n   236→\n   237→---\n   238→\n   239→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   240→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   241→\n   242→### Variable Mapping\n   243→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   244→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   245→| `spikes.times`, `spikes.clusters`, `clusters.metrics.label` from all probes in a frozen-release session | `neural` | merge probes by session after filtering to clusters with `label >= 1`, bin spike counts on a common stimulus-onset window `[-0.5, 1.5]` s with 20 ms bins, transpose to `(n_neurons, 100)` per trial | `prepare_data`, `merge_probes`, `bin_spiking_data`, `get_spike_data_per_interval` | `label >= 1` reproduces the paper’s 75,708 well-isolated units exactly |\n   246→| common trial grid relative to `stimOn_times` | `input[0]` = `time_since_stimulus_onset_s` | 100-length vector of relative times on the same grid as aligned behavior/neural bins; use a common vector for every trial | reference code uses `time_window` and `binsize`; behavior interpolation grid from `get_behavior_per_interval` | represent as time-varying continuous input |\n   247→| `probabilityLeft` block structure in raw trial table | `input[1]` = `trial_number_in_block` | compute block index from original unfiltered session trial sequence; reset to 1 whenever `probabilityLeft` changes; then repeat the scalar across all 100 bins for each kept trial | custom logic consistent with raw trial table semantics | unbiased 0.5 block counts as its own block |\n   248→| raw `choice` in trial table (`-1`, `1`) after filtering | `output[0]` = `choice` | map left `1 -> 0`, right `-1 -> 1`; repeat across all 100 bins for a common 2D output shape | `load_trials_and_mask`, `bin_behaviors` | verified directly from high-contrast correct trials; no-choice trials are removed before mapping |\n   249→| raw `probabilityLeft` in trial table (`0.2`, `0.5`, `0.8`) | `output[1]` = `prior_probability_of_left` | map `0.2 -> 0`, `0.5 -> 1`, `0.8 -> 2`; repeat across all 100 bins | `bin_behaviors` for `block` source; task-specific remapping | categorical per trial |\n   250→| wheel trace from `_ibl_wheel.*` via ONE/SessionLoader-equivalent logic | `output[2]` = `wheel_speed_bin` | compute wheel speed as absolute velocity; align/interpolate to the common stimulus-onset grid; discretize using global 3-bin thresholds; store category index `0/1/2` per time bin | `load_target_behavior('wheel-speed')`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | global thresholds shared across all sessions/trials for consistent categories |\n   251→| whisker motion energy from left camera, else right camera | `output[3]` = `whisker_motion_energy_bin` | load left whisker motion energy if available, else right; align/interpolate to common stimulus-onset grid; discretize with global 3-bin thresholds; store `0/1/2` per time bin | `load_target_behavior`, `bin_behaviors`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | do not mix left/right within a trial; session uses preferred available side |\n   252→| subject IDs from frozen release metadata | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | unique sorted subject list and per-session index | `bwm_release.csv` session metadata | order matches session order in converted dataset |\n   253→| cluster acronyms from probe cluster metadata | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | map each neuron acronym to Beryl acronym, build unique region list, store integer region index per neuron | `list_brain_regions` / `BrainRegions().acronym2acronym(..., mapping='Beryl')` | Beryl mapping matches reference region aggregation used in code |\n   254→| release/session metadata | `metadata` | include task description, 20 ms bin size, stimulus-onset alignment, offsets `-0.5/+1.5`, release source, discretization thresholds, modality notes | custom | keep enough detail for reproducibility |\n   255→\n   256→### Key Decisions\n   257→1. **Use the 459-session frozen BWM release as the source session set**: This matches both the data paper public release and `code/code_zhang2025/data/bwm_release.csv`.\n   258→1. **Require local existence of trials, wheel, and at least one whisker motion-energy stream**: All 459 frozen sessions exist locally; 439 have at least one whisker motion-energy stream, so sessions lacking whisker motion energy will be excluded because that output is mandatory for this decoder task.\n   259→1. **Use a single stimulus-onset-aligned 20 ms grid for every variable**: This satisfies the user’s explicit task requirement and simplifies the validator/decoder interface.\n   260→1. **Represent both inputs as 2D arrays of shape `(2, 100)`**: `time_since_stimulus_onset_s` varies over time; `trial_number_in_block` is repeated across time for each trial.\n   261→1. **Represent all outputs as 2D arrays of shape `(4, 100)`**: static outputs (`choice`, `prior`) are repeated across time so all outputs share a common structure with the dynamic outputs.\n   262→1. **Filter neurons to `label >= 1`, while preserving per-neuron QC metadata and Beryl region labels**: This matches the public-release well-isolated-neuron count of 75,708 exactly and keeps the dense pickle size compatible with the provided decoder.\n   263→1. **Compute `trial_number_in_block` on the original trial table before filtering**: This preserves the actual behavioural position within a block rather than renumbering after trial exclusion.\n   264→1. **Use left whisker motion energy when present, else right**: This matches the provided code and maximizes session retention.\n   265→1. **Discretize wheel speed and whisker motion energy with global 3-bin thresholds**: session-specific thresholds would make class labels inconsistent across sessions; global thresholds keep categories comparable.\n   266→1. **Drop any trial lacking full valid coverage of neural, wheel, or whisker data on the common window**: no padding or fabricated values.\n   267→\n   268→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   269→- [ ] Neural spot-check: for a chosen session/trial/neuron, recompute binned spike counts directly from raw `spikes.times` / `spikes.clusters` and verify equality with `np.allclose()`.\n   270→- [ ] Input spot-check: verify the converted `time_since_stimulus_onset_s` row equals the planned 100-bin relative-time vector and that `trial_number_in_block` matches direct computation from the raw trial table.\n   271→- [ ] Output spot-check (static): verify converted `choice` and `prior_probability_of_left` categories match raw `choice` and `probabilityLeft` on selected trials.\n   272→- [ ] Output spot-check (dynamic): verify a selected trial’s wheel-speed and whisker-motion-energy category sequences match direct raw interpolation followed by bin assignment using the saved global thresholds.\n   273→- [ ] Session-count sanity: compare number of included sessions and trials after mandatory modality/trial filtering against the 459-session release and the 433-session method-paper analysis subset.\n   274→- [ ] Neuron-count sanity: compare converted neuron counts both before and after optional QC summaries against the raw cluster counts and the paper’s well-isolated-neuron count.\n   275→\n   276→---\n   277→\n   278→## Step 6: Script Development\n   279→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   280→\n   281→- Installed `iblatlas` successfully so Beryl region mapping can match the reference code.\n   282→- Direct reuse of `brainbox.io.one.SessionLoader` is not viable in this environment because `brainbox.io.one` imports the unavailable `neuropixel` package, and `ONE.load_object(...)` against the local cache hits `.rest` permission issues.\n   283→- Conversion will therefore use direct ALF file loading plus the bundled `brainbox.behavior.wheel.interpolate_position` / `velocity_filtered` helpers and the bundled `brainbox.population.decode` logic where possible.\n   284→- Verified that filtering clusters by `label >= 1` across the 699 frozen-release insertions yields exactly 75,708 units, matching the data paper.\n   285→- Implemented `/app/convert_data.py` as a two-pass converter:\n   286→  - pass 1 loads trials + wheel + whisker, applies the reference trial mask plus behavior-coverage mask, and computes global dynamic-output tertile thresholds\n   287→  - pass 2 reloads good units (`label >= 1`), bins stimulus-aligned spikes into 20 ms bins, constructs decoder inputs/outputs, and writes the pickle\n   288→- Implemented direct Beryl region mapping from `channels.brainLocationIds_ccf_2017.npy` via `iblatlas.regions.BrainRegions`.\n   289→- Implemented reference-style video timestamp repair where camera timestamps longer than motion-energy arrays are trimmed from the front.\n   290→- Smoke test completed successfully:\n   291→  - command: `python3 -u /app/convert_data.py /app/_step6_smoke.pkl --sample`\n   292→  - result: 2 sessions, 651 trials, 340 well-isolated neurons, no runtime errors\n   293→\n   294→Code inefficiencies identified:\n   295→- Repeated recursive path discovery across revisioned ALF files would be slow if done inside inner trial loops.\n   296→- Dense float32 storage would be too large for the full release.\n   297→- Initial full-run profiling showed the real bottleneck was spike-stream reload and dtype copying in pass 2, not the trial-binning code.\n   298→\n   299→Code speedups added:\n   300→- Vectorized per-trial spike binning via `np.searchsorted` + flattened `np.bincount`.\n   301→- Low-precision storage for neural arrays (`float16`) to keep the dense pickle tractable while remaining compatible with the decoder validator.\n   302→- Behavior interpolation performed only once per session in pass 1; neural binning deferred until after global discretization thresholds are known.\n   303→- Session-level parallelism for full runs via `ThreadPoolExecutor`; sample / plotting runs stay single-worker for simpler debugging.\n   304→- BLAS/OpenMP thread counts are capped at 1 inside the converter so session-level parallelism is not drowned out by nested threading.\n   305→- Pass-2 spike loading now uses ALF-native cluster indexing, memory-mapped spike arrays, and no-copy dtype handling where possible; this removed the largest full-array copies.\n   306→- Merged spike times no longer use stable sort because within-timestamp probe ordering is irrelevant for binned counts.\n   307→- Full-run worker cap increased to 32 after confirming the machine has 64 CPUs and ample RAM.\n   308→\n   309→---\n   310→\n   311→## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   312→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   313→\n   314→### Sample
