`code/ibllib` is the library dependency used to access IBL data.\n- The reference pipeline is electrophysiology, not imaging. No delta-F/F computation is involved.\n- Reference caching parameters in `0_data_caching.py`:\n  - `align_time='stimOn_times'`\n  - `time_window=(-0.5, 1.5)` seconds\n  - `interval_len=2`\n  - `binsize=0.02` seconds\n- Therefore the nominal neural/behavior trial window is 2 s around stimulus onset, with 20 ms bins, yielding 100 bins.\n- Trial curation in `load_trials_and_mask` uses:\n  - reaction time between 0.08 s and 2.0 s\n  - trial duration (`feedback_times - goCue_times`) <= 10 s\n  - non-null `stimOn_times`, `choice`, `feedback_times`, `probabilityLeft`, `firstMovement_times`, `feedbackType`\n  - exclude no-choice trials (`choice == 0`)\n  - do not exclude unbiased block by default\n- Spike processing:\n  - probes are merged within session\n  - all clusters are loaded during caching (`qc=None`)\n  - cluster QC is still recorded in metadata as `good_clusters = (label >= 1)`\n  - no firing-rate smoothing; data are spike counts per bin\n- Behavior processing:\n  - `choice`, `block` (`probabilityLeft`), `reward`, and signed `contrast` are per-trial values\n  - `wheel-speed` is `abs(wheel velocity)`\n  - `whisker-motion-energy` prefers left camera and falls back to right camera if needed\n  - continuous streams are linearly interpolated within each trial-aligned window\n  - interpolation sample points are `linspace(interval_beg + binsize, interval_end, n_bins)`\n- Data orientation in the cached dataset is `(trial, time, neuron)` for spikes; decoder target format will need per-trial matrices transposed to `(neuron, time)`.\n- Potential discrepancy to revisit later: `align_spike_behavior` uses Python list `and` semantics and overwrites `beh_mask` within the loop, so the effective trial filtering logic may only reflect the last behavior plus `trials_mask`.\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n- Main raw data cache is under `data/one_cache/`.\n- Three registry snapshots are present:\n  - mouse reports by wheel turn\n  - 90 unbiased trials at 50:50, then alternating biased blocks with `probabilityLeft` 0.2 or 0.8\n  - 0% contrast trials follow block prior and still require a left/right response\n- Temporal alignment from method paper:\n  - choice: align to stimulus onset, use neural activity from -0.5 s to +1.5 s\n  - prior: align to stimulus onset, use neural activity from -0.6 s to -0.1 s\n  - wheel speed / whisker motion energy: align to first movement onset, decode from alignment event to +1.0 s\n- Temporal binning from method paper:\n  - 20 ms bins for choice and dynamic behaviors in the main formulation\n  - 50 ms bins specifically stated for prior\n- Additional decoding details from data paper:\n  - wheel speed / velocity decoded in non-overlapping 20 ms bins from -0.2 s to +1.0 s around first movement, with a causal window of W = 10 bins for regression\n  - binary variables evaluated with balanced accuracy\n  - continuous variables evaluated with R2\n- Motion energy definition from data paper:\n  - mean absolute pixel difference between adjacent frames in a whisker-pad bounding box anchored between nose tip and eye\n- Data architecture white paper:\n  - raw data are stored in ALF / ONE-style dataset types\n  - session-relative files and revisions are expected; this matches the versioned `alf/#...#/` files observed locally\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n- Public release kept 621,733 units including multineuron activity.\n- Main data-paper analyses further defined “well-isolated neurons” using RIGOR single-unit QC:\n  - amplitude > 50 uV\n  - noise cut-off < 20 uV\n  - refractory-period violation criterion\n- Final main analyses further restricted to grey-matter regions with at least 5 well-isolated neurons per session and at least 2 such sessions.\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Release / analysis trial exclusions in data paper:\n  - exclude trials missing `choice`, `probabilityLeft`, `feedbackType`, `feedback_times`, `stimOn_times`, or `firstMovement_times`\n  - exclude trials with reaction time (`firstMovement_times - stimOn_times`) outside 0.08–2.00 s\n- Sessions in public release had to satisfy:\n  - at least 250 trials\n  - at least 90% correct on 100% contrast trials for both left and right blocks\n  - at least 3 incorrect-choice trials after exclusions\n  - hardware QC threshold\n- Main paper also notes only sessions with at least 400 trials were retained for further analyses.\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n| Choice | multi-session behavioral-state model improves over baseline; example figure shows choice AUC improving from about 0.66 baseline to about 0.79 on an example session |\n| Prior | LG-AR1 improves over baseline; example figure shows prior correlation improving from about 0.05 baseline to about 0.34 multi-session and about 0.65 oracle on an example session |\n| Wheel speed | paper evaluates with R2; figures indicate multi-session RRR outperforms baseline / single-session models |\n| Whisker motion energy | paper evaluates with R2; figures indicate multi-session RRR outperforms baseline / single-session models |\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Session universe | `code/code_zhang2025/data/bwm_release.csv` contains 699 probe insertions / 459 unique sessions / 139 subjects; Ignore extra/newer sessions in local registry snapshots unless needed only for file lookup. |\n| Probe counts | code freeze implies 219 one-probe and 240 two-probe sessions | local folders show 221 one-probe and 240 two-probe session directories | data paper: “most recordings using 2 simultaneous probe insertions” and total 699 insertions | Use `bwm_release.csv` / probe metadata to define valid insertions for the target release; local folders appear to include extra sessions outside the frozen release. |\n| Method-paper session count | code examples do not mention 433 explicitly | local cache has enough sessions to support a large subset but not an exact 433 count from folders alone | method paper: 433 IBL sessions across 270 brain regions | Interpret 433 as a downstream analysis subset of the 459-session release after additional modality / analysis availability constraints. Preserve raw QC metadata in `metadata`. |\n| Trial filtering | code excludes missing `stimOn_times`, `choice`, `feedback_times`, `probabilityLeft`, `firstMovement_times`, `feedbackType`; RT outside 0.08–2.0 s; no-choice trials; plus `max_trial_len=10.0` | trial tables contain all required columns and some no-choice / NaN rows | data paper excludes the same missing events and RT 0.08–2.00 s; release sessions need >=250 trials; main analyses often >=400 trials | Mandatory filters are the shared missing-event and RT filters. Keep `exclude_nochoice=True` and `max_trial_len=10.0` to remain consistent with the provided code path, while noting these are stricter than the minimal text summary. |\n| Temporal alignment for `choice` | code cache aligns to `stimOn_times` with window `(-0.5, 1.5)` and 20 ms bins | raw data contain `stimOn_times` and `firstMovement_times` | method paper also aligns choice to stimulus onset | No discrepancy. Use stimulus-onset alignment for choice. |\n| Temporal alignment for `prior` | provided code cache does not special-case prior and would align to `stimOn_times` with the same 2 s / 20 ms window as other targets | raw data contain `probabilityLeft` per trial | method paper says prior uses stimulus onset but a different window (`-0.6` to `-0.1` s) and 50 ms bins | For this task, decoder inputs/outputs must share a common stimulus-onset-aligned trial grid. Use a common 20 ms stimulus-onset grid for the converted dataset, and document that this is a task-imposed simplification relative to the method paper’s prior-specific window. |\n| Temporal alignment for `wheel-speed` and `whisker-motion-energy` | provided code cache aligns all behaviors to `stimOn_times` | raw data provide continuous wheel and whisker streams plus `stimOn_times` and `firstMovement_times` | method paper says dynamic behaviors should align to `firstMovement_times` and run to +1 s | User explicitly requires “Temporally align based on stimulus onset.” Therefore, use stimulus-onset alignment for dynamic outputs as a deliberate task override, while keeping the reference code’s binning, interpolation style, and trial masking wherever possible. |\n| Binning of dynamic behavior | code uses 20 ms bins and linear interpolation inside each interval | raw wheel / motion-energy streams have irregular session-wide sampling (wheel) or camera-rate sampling (60 Hz / 150 Hz) | method paper dynamic behavior bins are 20 ms | No substantive discrepancy. Use 20 ms bins with trial-wise interpolation. |\n| Binning of prior | code examples / cache infrastructure assume 20 ms grids; prior notebooks treat prior as scalar over trials | `probabilityLeft` is available per trial; no continuous prior trace in raw data | method paper prior bins neural data at 50 ms and decodes a scalar per trial | Represent prior as per-trial categorical output on the common trial grid in the converted dataset, duplicating across time if needed for shape consistency only if required later. |\n| Motion-energy source | code prefers left whisker motion energy and falls back to right camera if missing | 434 local sessions have left motion energy; 421 have right; 441 have at least one side | data paper computes whisker-pad motion energy from left and right videos | Prefer left camera when available, else right, matching the provided code and the local coverage pattern. |\n| Data organization | code assumes ONE / ALF loading with revisions and dataset types | local files are indeed stored under revisioned `alf/#...#/` paths and `probeXX/pykilosort/...` | data architecture paper describes ONE / ALF dataset-type conventions | Follow ALF / ONE naming and revision handling already present in local cache. |\n\nFinal understanding:\n- Start from the frozen 459-session / 699-insertion BWM release, not from all locally visible session folders.\n- Apply the shared trial-quality filters from the papers plus the provided code’s `exclude_nochoice=True` and `max_trial_len=10.0`.\n- Use `label >= 1` well-isolated units because this reproduces the paper’s 75,708-neuron statistic exactly and is the release-consistent dense representation that remains computationally tractable for the provided decoder.\n- Use a common 20 ms stimulus-onset-aligned grid for the converted dataset because the user explicitly requires stimulus-onset alignment and the target decoder needs common trial shapes across neural, input, and output streams.\n- Treat method-paper target-specific alignment differences (especially dynamic behaviors and prior) as important reference context, but as overridden where they conflict with the user’s explicit decoder specification.\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `spikes.times`, `spikes.clusters`, `clusters.metrics.label` from all probes in a frozen-release session | `neural` | merge probes by session after filtering to clusters with `label >= 1`, bin spike counts on a common stimulus-onset window `[-0.5, 1.5]` s with 20 ms bins, transpose to `(n_neurons, 100)` per trial | `prepare_data`, `merge_probes`, `bin_spiking_data`, `get_spike_data_per_interval` | `label >= 1` reproduces the paper’s 75,708 well-isolated units exactly |\n| common trial grid relative to `stimOn_times` | `input[0]` = `time_since_stimulus_onset_s` | 100-length vector of relative times on the same grid as aligned behavior/neural bins; use a common vector for every trial | reference code uses `time_window` and `binsize`; behavior interpolation grid from `get_behavior_per_interval` | represent as time-varying continuous input |\n| `probabilityLeft` block structure in raw trial table | `input[1]` = `trial_number_in_block` | compute block index from original unfiltered session trial sequence; reset to 1 whenever `probabilityLeft` changes; then repeat the scalar across all 100 bins for each kept trial | custom logic consistent with raw trial table semantics | unbiased 0.5 block counts as its own block |\n| raw `choice` in trial table (`-1`, `1`) after filtering | `output[0]` = `choice` | map left `1 -> 0`, right `-1 -> 1`; repeat across all 100 bins for a common 2D output shape | `load_trials_and_mask`, `bin_behaviors` | verified directly from high-contrast correct trials; no-choice trials are removed before mapping |\n| raw `probabilityLeft` in trial table (`0.2`, `0.5`, `0.8`) | `output[1]` = `prior_probability_of_left` | map `0.2 -> 0`, `0.5 -> 1`, `0.8 -> 2`; repeat across all 100 bins | `bin_behaviors` for `block` source; task-specific remapping | categorical per trial |\n| wheel trace from `_ibl_wheel.*` via ONE/SessionLoader-equivalent logic | `output[2]` = `wheel_speed_bin` | compute wheel speed as absolute velocity; align/interpolate to the common stimulus-onset grid; discretize using global 3-bin thresholds; store category index `0/1/2` per time bin | `load_target_behavior('wheel-speed')`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | global thresholds shared across all sessions/trials for consistent categories |\n| whisker motion energy from left camera, else right camera | `output[3]` = `whisker_motion_energy_bin` | load left whisker motion energy if available, else right; align/interpolate to common stimulus-onset grid; discretize with global 3-bin thresholds; store `0/1/2` per time bin | `load_target_behavior`, `bin_behaviors`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | do not mix left/right within a trial; session uses preferred available side |\n| subject IDs from frozen release metadata | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | unique sorted subject list and per-session index | `bwm_release.csv` session metadata | order matches session order in converted dataset |\n| cluster acronyms from probe cluster metadata | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | map each neuron acronym to Beryl acronym, build unique region list, store integer region index per neuron | `list_brain_regions` / `BrainRegions().acronym2acronym(..., mapping='Beryl')` | Beryl mapping matches reference region aggregation used in code |\n| release/session metadata | `metadata` | include task description, 20 ms bin size, stimulus-onset alignment, offsets `-0.5/+1.5`, release source, discretization thresholds, modality notes | custom | keep enough detail for reproducibility |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Use the 459-session frozen BWM release as the source session set**: This matches both the data paper public release and `code/code_zhang2025/data/bwm_release.csv`.\n1. **Require local existence of trials, wheel, and at least one whisker motion-energy stream**: All 459 frozen sessions exist locally; 439 have at least one whisker motion-energy stream, so sessions lacking whisker motion energy will be excluded because that output is mandatory for this decoder task.\n1. **Use a single stimulus-onset-aligned 20 ms grid for every variable**: This satisfies the user’s explicit task requirement and simplifies the validator/decoder interface.\n1. **Represent both inputs as 2D arrays of shape `(2, 100)`**: `time_since_stimulus_onset_s` varies over time; `trial_number_in_block` is repeated across time for each trial.\n1. **Represent all outputs as 2D arrays of shape `(4, 100)`**: static outputs (`choice`, `prior`) are repeated across time so all outputs share a common structure with the dynamic outputs.\n1. **Filter neurons to `label >= 1`, while preserving per-neuron QC metadata and Beryl region labels**: This matches the public-release well-isolated-neuron count of 75,708 exactly and keeps the dense pickle size compatible with the provided decoder.\n1. **Compute `trial_number_in_block` on the original trial table before filtering**: This preserves the actual behavioural position within a block rather than renumbering after trial exclusion.\n1. **Use left whisker motion energy when present, else right**: This matches the provided code and maximizes session retention.\n1. **Discretize wheel speed and whisker motion energy with global 3-bin thresholds**: session-specific thresholds would make class labels inconsistent across sessions; global thresholds keep categories comparable.\n1. **Drop any trial lacking full valid coverage of neural, wheel, or whisker data on the common window**: no padding or fabricated values.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Neural spot-check: for a chosen session/trial/neuron, recompute binned spike counts directly from raw `spikes.times` / `spikes.clusters` and verify equality with `np.allclose()`.\n- [ ] Input spot-check: verify the converted `time_since_stimulus_onset_s` row equals the planned 100-bin relative-time vector and that `trial_number_in_block` matches direct computation from the raw trial table.\n- [ ] Output spot-check (static): verify converted `choice` and `prior_probability_of_left` categories match raw `choice` and `probabilityLeft` on selected trials.\n- [ ] Output spot-check (dynamic): verify a selected trial’s wheel-speed and whisker-motion-energy category sequences match direct raw interpolation followed by bin assignment using the saved global thresholds.\n- [ ] Session-count sanity: compare number of included sessions and trials after mandatory modality/trial filtering against the 459-session release and the 433-session method-paper analysis subset.\n- [ ] Neuron-count sanity: compare converted neuron counts both before and after optional QC summaries against the raw cluster counts and the paper’s well-isolated-neuron count.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n- Installed `iblatlas` successfully so Beryl region mapping can match the reference code.\n- Direct reuse of `brainbox.io.one.SessionLoader` is not viable in this environment because `brainbox.io.one` imports the unavailable `neuropixel` package, and `ONE.load_object(...)` against the local cache hits `.rest` permission issues.\n- Conversion will therefore use direct ALF file loading plus the bundled `brainbox.behavior.wheel.interpolate_position` / `velocity_filtered` helpers and the bundled `brainbox.population.decode` logic where possible.\n- Verified that filtering clusters by `label >= 1` across the 699 frozen-release insertions yields exactly 75,708 units, matching the data paper.\n- Implemented `/app/convert_data.py` as a two-pass converter:\n  - pass 1 loads trials + wheel + whisker, applies the reference trial mask plus behavior-coverage mask, and computes global dynamic-output tertile thresholds\n  - pass 2 reloads good units (`label >= 1`), bins stimulus-aligned spikes into 20 ms bins, constructs decoder inputs/outputs, and writes the pickle\n- Implemented direct Beryl region mapping from `channels.brainLocationIds_ccf_2017.npy` via `iblatlas.regions.BrainRegions`.\n- Implemented reference-style video timestamp repair where camera timestamps longer than motion-energy arrays are trimmed from the front.\n- Smoke test completed successfully:\n  - command: `python3 -u /app/convert_data.py /app/_step6_smoke.pkl --sample`\n  - result: 2 sessions, 651 trials, 340 well-isolated neurons, no runtime errors\n\nCode inefficiencies identified:\n- Repeated recursive path discovery across revisioned ALF files would be slow if done inside inner trial loops.\n- Dense float32 storage would be too large for the full release.\n- Initial full-run profiling showed the real bottleneck was spike-stream reload and dtype copying in pass 2, not the trial-binning code.\n\nCode speedups added:\n- Vectorized per-trial spike binning via `np.searchsorted` + flattened `np.bincount`.\n- Low-precision storage for neural arrays (`float16`) to keep the dense pickle tractable while remaining compatible with the decoder validator.\n- Behavior interpolation performed only once per session in pass 1; neural binning deferred until after global discretization thresholds are known.\n- Session-level parallelism for full runs via `ThreadPoolExecutor`; sample / plotting runs stay single-worker for simpler debugging.\n- BLAS/OpenMP thread counts are capped at 1 inside the converter so session-level parallelism is not drowned out by nested threading.\n- Pass-2 spike loading now uses ALF-native cluster indexing, memory-mapped spike arrays, and no-copy dtype handling where possible; this removed the largest full-array copies.\n- Merged spike times no longer use stable sort because within-timestamp probe ordering is irrelevant for binned counts.\n- Full-run worker cap increased to 32 after confirming the machine has 64 CPUs and ample RAM.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample can QC-filter if `qc` is passed, but reference caching calls it with `qc=None`. |\n    35→| `merge_probes` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Reindexes cluster IDs and concatenates/sorts spikes across probes to make a session-wide spike train. |\n    36→| `load_trials_and_mask` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | CURATION | Builds a boolean trial inclusion mask based on reaction time, trial length, missing key events, and no-choice trials. |\n    37→| `list_brain_regions` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Maps cluster acronyms to Beryl atlas acronyms and enumerates regions used for decoding. |\n    38→| `select_brain_regions` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | CURATION | Selects cluster IDs belonging to the requested region set. |\n    39→| `bin_spiking_data` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Converts session-wide spikes to trial-aligned binned spike-count tensors using `stimOn_times` and the configured time window. |\n    40→| `get_spike_data_per_interval` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Performs multi-bin spike counting per interval; uses `ceil(interval_len / binsize)` bins. |\n    41→| `load_target_behavior` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | LOADING | Loads continuous behavior streams such as wheel speed and whisker motion energy from session objects. |\n    42→| `get_behavior_per_interval` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Trial-aligns and linearly interpolates continuous behavior signals to the same interval and nominal bin count as neural data. |\n    43→| `bin_behaviors` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Constructs per-trial categorical variables (`choice`, `block`, `reward`, `contrast`) and time-varying behavior arrays (`wheel-speed`, `whisker-motion-energy`, etc.). |\n    44→| `align_spike_behavior` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | CURATION | Drops trials with missing behavior / excluded mask and reshapes behavior arrays so trial counts match spike arrays. |\n    45→| `create_dataset` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/dataset_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Serializes per-trial binned spikes plus behavior and cluster metadata into the cached dataset structure. |\n    46→| `SingleSessionDataset` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/data_loader_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Loads cached trials, reconstructs sparse spikes, standardizes spikes, one-hot encodes classification targets, and standardizes regression targets. |\n    47→| `0_data_caching.py` main loop | `code/code_zhang2025/src/0_data_caching.py` | LOADING | Reference end-to-end preprocessing entrypoint for selected sessions. |\n    48→\n    49→### Notes\n    50→- Relevant reference repository is `code/code_zhang2025`; `code/ibllib` is the library dependency used to access IBL data.\n    51→- The reference pipeline is electrophysiology, not imaging. No delta-F/F computation is involved.\n    52→- Reference caching parameters in `0_data_caching.py`:\n    53→  - `align_time='stimOn_times'`\n    54→  - `time_window=(-0.5, 1.5)` seconds\n    55→  - `interval_len=2`\n    56→  - `binsize=0.02` seconds\n    57→- Therefore the nominal neural/behavior trial window is 2 s around stimulus onset, with 20 ms bins, yielding 100 bins.\n    58→- Trial curation in `load_trials_and_mask` uses:\n    59→  - reaction time between 0.08 s and 2.0 s\n    60→  - trial duration (`feedback_times - goCue_times`) <= 10 s\n    61→  - non-null `stimOn_times`, `choice`, `feedback_times`, `probabilityLeft`, `firstMovement_times`, `feedbackType`\n    62→  - exclude no-choice trials (`choice == 0`)\n    63→  - do not exclude unbiased block by default\n    64→- Spike processing:\n    65→  - probes are merged within session\n    66→  - all clusters are loaded during caching (`qc=None`)\n    67→  - cluster QC is still recorded in metadata as `good_clusters = (label >= 1)`\n    68→  - no firing-rate smoothing; data are spike counts per bin\n    69→- Behavior processing:\n    70→  - `choice`, `block` (`probabilityLeft`), `reward`, and signed `contrast` are per-trial values\n    71→  - `wheel-speed` is `abs(wheel velocity)`\n    72→  - `whisker-motion-energy` prefers left camera and falls back to right camera if needed\n    73→  - continuous streams are linearly interpolated within each trial-aligned window\n    74→  - interpolation sample points are `linspace(interval_beg + binsize, interval_end, n_bins)`\n    75→- Data orientation in the cached dataset is `(trial, time, neuron)` for spikes; decoder target format will need per-trial matrices transposed to `(neuron, time)`.\n    76→- Potential discrepancy to revisit later: `align_spike_behavior` uses Python list `and` semantics and overwrites `beh_mask` within the loop, so the effective trial filtering logic may only reflect the last behavior plus `trials_mask`.\n    77→\n    78→---\n    79→\n    80→## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n    81→**Status**: COMPLETE\n    82→\n    83→### Data Structure\n    84→- Main raw data cache is under `data/one_cache/`.\n    85→- 160→- Task structure from paper:\n   161→  - visual stimulus left/right; mouse reports by wheel turn\n   162→  - 90 unbiased trials at 50:50, then alternating biased blocks with `probabilityLeft` 0.2 or 0.8\n   163→  - 0% contrast trials follow block prior and still require a left/right response\n   164→- Temporal alignment from method paper:\n   165→  - choice: align to stimulus onset, use neural activity from -0.5 s to +1.5 s\n   166→  - prior: align to stimulus onset, use neural activity from -0.6 s to -0.1 s\n   167→  - wheel speed / whisker motion energy: align to first movement onset, decode from alignment event to +1.0 s\n   168→- Temporal binning from method paper:\n   169→  - 20 ms bins for choice and dynamic behaviors in the main formulation\n   170→  - 50 ms bins specifically stated for prior\n   171→- Additional decoding details from data paper:\n   172→  - wheel speed / velocity decoded in non-overlapping 20 ms bins from -0.2 s to +1.0 s around first movement, with a causal window of W = 10 bins for regression\n   173→  - binary variables evaluated with balanced accuracy\n   174→  - continuous variables evaluated with R2\n   175→- Motion energy definition from data paper:\n   176→  - mean absolute pixel difference between adjacent frames in a whisker-pad bounding box anchored between nose tip and eye\n   177→- Data architecture white paper:\n   178→  - raw data are stored in ALF / ONE-style dataset types\n   179→  - session-relative files and revisions are expected; this matches the versioned `alf/#...#/` files observed locally\n   180→\n   181→### Curation Steps\n   182→\n   183→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   184→- Public release kept 621,733 units including multineuron activity.\n   185→- Main data-paper analyses further defined “well-isolated neurons” using RIGOR single-unit QC:\n   186→  - amplitude > 50 uV\n   187→  - noise cut-off < 20 uV\n   188→  - refractory-period violation criterion\n   189→- Final main analyses further restricted to grey-matter regions with at least 5 well-isolated neurons per session and at least 2 such sessions.\n   190→\n   191→**Trial curation rules**:\n   192→- Release / analysis trial exclusions in data paper:\n   193→  - exclude trials missing `choice`, `probabilityLeft`, `feedbackType`, `feedback_times`, `stimOn_times`, or `firstMovement_times`\n   194→  - exclude trials with reaction time (`firstMovement_times - stimOn_times`) outside 0.08–2.00 s\n   195→- Sessions in public release had to satisfy:\n   196→  - at least 250 trials\n   197→  - at least 90% correct on 100% contrast trials for both left and right blocks\n   198→  - at least 3 incorrect-choice trials after exclusions\n   199→  - hardware QC threshold\n   200→- Main paper also notes only sessions with at least 400 trials were retained for further analyses.\n   201→\n   202→### Decoders Trained\n   203→| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n   204→| Choice | multi-session behavioral-state model improves over baseline; example figure shows choice AUC improving from about 0.66 baseline to about 0.79 on an example session |\n   205→| Prior | LG-AR1 improves over baseline; example figure shows prior correlation improving from about 0.05 baseline to about 0.34 multi-session and about 0.65 oracle on an example session |\n   206→| Wheel speed | paper evaluates with R2; figures indicate multi-session RRR outperforms baseline / single-session models |\n   207→| Whisker motion energy | paper evaluates with R2; figures indicate multi-session RRR outperforms baseline / single-session models |\n   208→\n output on the common trial grid in the converted dataset, duplicating across time if needed for shape consistency only if required later. |\n   227→| Motion-energy source | code prefers left whisker Follow ALF / ONE naming and revision handling already present in local cache. |\n   229→\n   230→Final understanding:\n   231→- Start from the frozen 459-session / 699-insertion BWM release, not from Variable Mapping\n   243→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   244→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   245→| `spikes.times`, `spikes.clusters`, `clusters.metrics.label` from all probes in a frozen-release common trial grid relative to `stimOn_times` | `input[0]` = `time_since_stimulus_onset_s` | 100-length vector of relative times on the same grid as aligned behavior/neural bins; use a common vector for every trial | reference code uses `time_window` and `binsize`; behavior interpolation grid from `get_behavior_per_interval` | represent as time-varying continuous input |\n   247→| `probabilityLeft` block structure in raw trial table | `input[1]` = `trial_number_in_block` | compute block index from original unfiltered session trial sequence; reset to 1 whenever `probabilityLeft` changes; then repeat the scalar across all 100 bins for each kept trial | custom logic consistent with raw trial table semantics | unbiased 0.5 block counts as its own block |\n   248→| raw `choice` in trial table (`-1`, `1`) after filtering | `output[0]` = `choice` | map left `1 -> 0`, right `-1 -> 1`; repeat across all 100 bins for a common 2D output shape | `load_trials_and_mask`, = `prior_probability_of_left` | map `0.2 -> 0`, `0.5 -> 1`, `0.8 -> 2`; repeat across all 100 bins | `bin_behaviors` for `block` source; task-specific remapping | categorical per trial |\n   250→| wheel trace from `_ibl_wheel.*` via ONE/SessionLoader-equivalent logic | `output[2]` = `wheel_speed_bin` | compute wheel speed as absolute velocity; align/interpolate to the common stimulus-onset grid; discretize using global 3-bin thresholds; store category index `0/1/2` per time bin | `load_target_behavior('wheel-speed')`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | global thresholds shared across all sessions/trials for consistent categories |\n   251→| whisker motion energy from left camera, else right camera | `output[3]` = `whisker_motion_energy_bin` | load left whisker motion energy if available, else right; align/interpolate to common stimulus-onset grid; discretize with global 3-bin thresholds; store `0/1/2` per time bin | `load_target_behavior`, `bin_behaviors`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | do not mix left/right within a trial; session uses preferred available side |\n   252→| subject IDs from frozen release metadata | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | unique sorted subject list and per-session index | `bwm_release.csv` session metadata | order matches session order in converted dataset |\n   253→| cluster acronyms from probe cluster metadata | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | map each neuron acronym to Beryl acronym, build unique region list, store integer region index per neuron | `list_brain_regions` / `BrainRegions().acronym2acronym(..., mapping='Beryl')` | Beryl mapping matches reference region aggregation used in code |\n   254→| release/session metadata | `metadata` | include task description, 20 ms bin size, stimulus-onset alignment, offsets `-0.5/+1.5`, release source, discretization thresholds, modality notes | custom | keep enough detail for reproducibility |\n   255→\n   256→### Key Decisions\n   257→1. **Use the 459-session frozen BWM release as the source session set**: This matches both the data paper public release and `code/code_zhang2025/data/bwm_release.csv`.\n   258→1. **Require local existence of trials, wheel, and at least one whisker motion-energy stream**: All 459 frozen sessions exist locally; 439 have at least one whisker motion-energy stream, so sessions lacking whisker motion energy will be excluded because that output is mandatory for this decoder task.\n   259→1. **Use a single stimulus-onset-aligned 20 ms grid for every variable**: This satisfies the user’s explicit task requirement and simplifies the validator/decoder interface.\n   260→1. **Represent both inputs as 2D arrays of shape `(2, 100)`**: `time_since_stimulus_onset_s` varies over time; `trial_number_in_block` is repeated across time for each trial.\n   261→1. **Represent all outputs as 2D arrays of shape `(4, 100)`**: static outputs (`choice`, `prior`) are repeated across time so all outputs share a common structure with the dynamic outputs.\n   262→1. **Filter neurons to `label >= 1`, while preserving per-neuron QC metadata and Beryl region labels**: This matches the public-release well-isolated-neuron count of 75,708 exactly and keeps the dense pickle size compatible with the provided decoder.\n   263→1. **Compute `trial_number_in_block` on the original trial table before filtering**: This preserves the actual behavioural position within a block rather than renumbering after trial exclusion.\n   264→1. **Use left whisker motion energy when present, else right**: This matches the provided code and maximizes session retention.\n   265→1. **Discretize wheel speed and whisker motion energy with global 3-bin thresholds**: session-specific thresholds would make class labels inconsistent across sessions; global thresholds keep categories comparable.\n   266→1. **Drop any trial lacking full valid coverage of neural, wheel, or whisker data on the common window**: no padding or fabricated values.\n   267→\n   268→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   269→- [ ] Neural spot-check: for a chosen session/trial/neuron, recompute binned spike counts directly from raw `spikes.times` / `spikes.clusters` and verify equality with `np.allclose()`.\n   270→- [ ] Input spot-check: verify the converted `time_since_stimulus_onset_s` row equals the planned 100-bin relative-time vector and that `trial_number_in_block` matches direct computation from the raw trial table.\n   271→- [ ] Output spot-check (static): verify converted `choice` and `prior_probability_of_left` categories match raw `choice` and `probabilityLeft` on selected trials.\n   272→- [ ] Output spot-check (dynamic): verify a selected trial’s wheel-speed and whisker-motion-energy category sequences match direct raw interpolation followed by bin assignment using the saved global thresholds.\n   273→- [ ] Session-count sanity: compare number of included sessions and trials after mandatory modality/trial filtering against the 459-session release and the 433-session method-paper analysis subset.\n   274→- [ ] Neuron-count sanity: compare converted neuron counts both before and after optional QC summaries against the raw cluster counts and the paper’s well-isolated-neuron count.\n   275→\n   276→---\n   277→\n   278→## Step 6: Script Development\n   279→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   280→\n   281→- Installed `iblatlas` successfully so Beryl region mapping can match the reference code.\n   282→- Direct reuse of `brainbox.io.one.SessionLoader` is not viable in this environment because `brainbox.io.one` imports the unavailable `neuropixel` package, and `ONE.load_object(...)` against the local cache hits `.rest` permission issues.\n   283→- Conversion will therefore use direct ALF file loading plus the bundled `brainbox.behavior.wheel.interpolate_position` / `velocity_filtered` helpers and the bundled `brainbox.population.decode` logic where possible.\n   284→- Verified that filtering clusters by `label >= 1` across the 699 frozen-release insertions yields exactly 75,708 units, matching the data paper.\n   285→- Implemented `/app/convert_data.py` as a two-pass converter:\n   286→  - pass 1 loads trials + wheel + whisker, applies the reference trial mask plus behavior-coverage mask, and computes global dynamic-output tertile thresholds\n   287→  - pass 2 reloads good units (`label >= 1`), bins stimulus-aligned spikes into 20 ms bins, constructs decoder inputs/outputs, and writes the pickle\n   288→- Implemented direct Beryl region mapping from `channels.brainLocationIds_ccf_2017.npy` via `iblatlas.regions.BrainRegions`.\n   289→- Implemented reference-style video timestamp repair where camera timestamps longer than motion-energy arrays are trimmed from the front.\n   290→- Smoke test completed successfully:\n   291→  - command: `python3 -u /app/convert_data.py /app/_step6_smoke.pkl --sample`\n   292→  - result: 2 sessions, 651 trials, 340 well-isolated neurons, no runtime errors\n   293→\n   294→Code inefficiencies identified:\n   295→- Repeated recursive path discovery across revisioned ALF files would be slow if done inside inner trial loops.\n   296→- Dense float32 storage would be too large for the full release.\n   297→- Initial full-run profiling showed the real bottleneck was spike-stream reload and dtype copying in pass 2, not the trial-binning code.\n   298→\n   299→Code speedups added:\n   300→- Vectorized per-trial spike binning via `np.searchsorted` + flattened `np.bincount`.\n   301→- Low-precision storage for neural arrays (`float16`) to keep the dense pickle tractable while remaining compatible with the decoder validator.\n   302→- Behavior interpolation performed only once per session in pass 1; neural binning deferred until after global discretization thresholds are known.\n   303→- Session-level parallelism for full runs via `ThreadPoolExecutor`; sample / plotting runs stay single-worker for simpler debugging.\n   304→- BLAS/OpenMP thread counts are capped at 1 inside the converter so session-level parallelism is not drowned out by nested threading.\n   305→- Pass-2 spike loading now uses ALF-native cluster indexing, memory-mapped spike arrays, and no-copy dtype handling where possible; this removed the largest full-array copies.\n   306→- Merged spike times no longer use stable sort because within-timestamp probe ordering is irrelevant for binned counts.\n   307→- Full-run worker cap increased to 32 after confirming the machine has 64 CPUs and ample RAM.\n   308→\n   309→---\n   310→\n   311→## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   312→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   313→\n   314→### Sample return timestamps.astype(np.float64), motion_energy.astype(np.float32), view\n   213→    raise FileNotFoundError(f\"No whisker motion energy stream found for {session_path}\")\n   214→\n   215→\n   216→def interpolate_behavior_per_trial(\n   217→    target_times: np.ndarray,\n   218→    target_vals: np.ndarray,\n   219→    align_times: np.ndarray,\n   220→    binsize: float = BINSIZE,\n   221→    time_window: tuple[float, float] = TIME_WINDOW,\n   222→) -> tuple[list[np.ndarray | None], np.ndarray]:\n   223→    interval_begs = align_times + time_window[0]\n   224→    interval_ends = align_times + time_window[1]\n   225→    n_bins = int(np.ceil((time_window[1] - time_window[0]) / binsize))\n   226→\n   227→    idxs_beg = np.searchsorted(target_times, interval_begs, side=\"right\")\n   228→    idxs_end = np.searchsorted(target_times, interval_ends, side=\"left\")\n   229→\n   230→    outputs: list[np.ndarray | None] = [None] * len(align_times)\n   231→    mask = np.zeros(len(align_times), dtype=bool)\n   232→\n   233→    for i in range(len(align_times)):\n   234→        t_beg = interval_begs[i]\n   235→        t_end = interval_ends[i]\n   236→        if np.isnan(t_beg) or np.isnan(t_end):\n   237→            continue\n   238→\n   239→        curr_times = target_times[idxs_beg[i]:idxs_end[i]]\n   240→        curr_vals = target_vals[idxs_beg[i]:idxs_end[i]]\n   241→        if curr_vals.shape[0] == 0:\n   242→            continue\n   243→        if np.isnan(curr_vals).any():\n   244→            continue\n   245→        if abs(t_beg - curr_times[0]) > binsize:\n   246→            continue\n   247→        if abs(t_end - curr_times[-1]) > binsize:\n   248→            continue\n   249→\n   250→        x_interp = np.linspace(t_beg + binsize, t_end, n_bins)\n   251→        y_interp = np.interp(x_interp, curr_times, curr_vals)\n   252→        outputs[i] = y_interp.astype(np.float32)\n   253→        mask[i] = True\n   254→\n   255→    return outputs, mask\n   256→\n   257→\n   258→def build_session_behavior(spec: SessionSpec) -> PreparedSession | None:\n   259→    trials = load_trials_table(spec.session_path)\n   260→    trial_mask = compute_trial_mask(trials)\n   261→    trial_number_in_block = compute_trial_number_in_block(trials[\"probabilityLeft\"].to_numpy())\n   262→    n_good_units = count_good_units(spec)\n   263→    if n_good_units == 0:\n   264→        return None\n   265→\n   266→    wheel_times, wheel_speed = load_wheel_speed(spec.session_path)\n   267→    whisker_times, whisker_motion, whisker_source = load_whisker_motion_energy(spec.session_path)\n   268→\n   269→    align_times = trials[ALIGN_EVENT].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)\n   270→    wheel_interp, wheel_mask = interpolate_behavior_per_trial(wheel_times, wheel_speed, align_times)\n   271→    whisker_interp, whisker_mask = interpolate_behavior_per_trial(whisker_times, whisker_motion, align_times)\n   272→\n   273→    keep_mask = trial_mask & wheel_mask & whisker_mask\n   274→    if keep_mask.sum() < 2:\n   275→        return None\n   276→\n   277→    wheel_cont = np.stack([wheel_interp[i] for i in np.where(keep_mask)[0]], axis=0)\n   278→    whisker_cont = np.stack([whisker_interp[i] for i in np.where(keep_mask)[0]], axis=0)\n   279→\n   280→    return PreparedSession(\n   281→        spec=spec,\n   282→        trials=trials,\n   283→        keep_mask=keep_mask,\n   284→        trial_number_in_block=trial_number_in_block[keep_mask],\n   285→        choice_raw=trials.loc[keep_mask, \"choice\"].to_numpy(),\n   286→        prior_raw=trials.loc[keep_mask, \"probabilityLeft\"].to_numpy(),\n   287→        wheel_cont=wheel_cont,\n   288→        whisker_cont=whisker_cont,\n   289→        n_good_units=n_good_units,\n   290→        whisker_source=whisker_source,\n   291→    )\n   292→\n   293→\n   294→def build_session_behavior_safe(spec: SessionSpec) -> tuple[str, PreparedSession | None, str | None]:\n   295→    try:\n   296→        prepared = build_session_behavior(spec)\n   297→        if prepared is None:\n   298→            return spec.eid, None, \"no_good_units_or_too_few_valid_trials\"\n   299→        return spec.eid, prepared, None\n   300→    except FileNotFoundError:\n   301→        return spec.eid, None, \"missing_required_stream\"\n   302→    except Exception as exc:  # pragma: no cover - defensive for raw-data irregularities\n   303→        return spec.eid, None, f\"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}\"\n   304→\n   305→\n   306→def robust_tertile_edges(values: np.ndarray) -> tuple[float, float]:\n   307→    finite = np.asarray(values, dtype=np.float32)\n   308→    finite = finite[np.isfinite(finite)]\n   309→    if finite.size == 0:\n   310→        return 0.0, 0.0\n   311→    q1, q2 = np.quantile(finite, [1 / 3, 2 / 3])\n   312→    if q2 > q1:\n   313→        return float(q1), float(q2)\n   314→\n   315→    sorted_vals = np.sort(finite)\n   316→    i1 = max(0, min(sorted_vals.size - 1, sorted_vals.size // 3))\n   317→    i2 = max(0, min(sorted_vals.size - 1, (2 * sorted_vals.size) // 3))\n   318→    q1 = float(sorted_vals[i1])\n   319→    q2 = float(sorted_vals[i2])\n   320→    if q2 <= q1:\n   321→        larger = sorted_vals[sorted_vals > q1]\n   322→        q2 = float(larger[0]) if larger.size else q1\n   323→    return q1, q2\n   324→\n   325→\n   326→def digitize_three_bins(values: np.ndarray, edges: tuple[float, float]) -> np.ndarray:\n   327→    low, high = edges\n   328→    if high <= low:\n   329→        return np.zeros(values.shape, dtype=np.int8)\n   330→    return np.digitize(values, bins=np.array([low, high], dtype=np.float32), right=False).astype(np.int8)\n   331→\n   332→\n   333→def load_good_spikes_and_regions(spec: SessionSpec, brain_regions: BrainRegions) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n   334→    spike_times_all: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   335→    spike_clusters_all: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   336→    cluster_regions_all: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   337→    cluster_offset = 0\n   338→\n   339→    for probe_name in spec.probe_names:\n   340→        probe_dir = spec.session_path / \"alf\" / probe_name / \"pykilosort\"\n   341→        metrics_path = resolve_latest(probe_dir, \"**/clusters.metrics.pqt\")\n   342→        clusters_channels_path = resolve_latest(probe_dir, \"**/clusters.channels.npy\")\n   343→        channels_region_ids_path = resolve_latest(probe_dir, \"**/channels.brainLocationIds_ccf_2017.npy\")\n   344→        spikes_times_path = resolve_latest(probe_dir, \"**/spikes.times.npy\")\n   345→        spikes_clusters_path = resolve_latest(probe_dir, \"**/spikes.clusters.npy\")\n   346→\n   347→        metrics = pd.read_parquet(metrics_path, columns=[\"label\"])\n   348→        good_rows = metrics[\"label\"].to_numpy(copy=False) >= 1\n   349→        if not np.any(good_rows):\n   350→            continue\n   351→\n   352→        spikes_times = np.load(spikes_times_path, mmap_mode=\"r\")\n   353→        spikes_clusters = np.load(spikes_clusters_path, mmap_mode=\"r\")\n   354→        n_clusters = good_rows.shape[0]\n   355→        valid_spikes = spikes_clusters < n_clusters\n   356→        if np.all(valid_spikes):\n   357→            spike_mask = good_rows[spikes_clusters]\n   358→        else:\n   359→            spike_mask = np.zeros(spikes_clusters.shape[0], dtype=bool)\n   360→            spike_mask[valid_spikes] = good_rows[spikes_clusters[valid_spikes]]\n   361→        remap = np.full(n_clusters, -1, dtype=np.int32)\n   362→        remap[good_rows] = np.arange(int(good_rows.sum()), dtype=np.int32)\n   363→        good_spike_times = np.asarray(spikes_times[spike_mask], dtype=np.float64)\n   364→        good_spike_clusters = remap[spikes_clusters[spike_mask]] + cluster_offset\n   365→\n   366→        cluster_channels = np.load(clusters_channels_path, mmap_mode=\"r\")[good_rows]\n   367→        channel_region_ids = np.load(channels_region_ids_path, mmap_mode=\"r\")\n   368→        region_ids = np.zeros(cluster_channels.shape[0], dtype=np.int64)\n   369→        valid_channel = (cluster_channels >= 0) & (cluster_channels < channel_region_ids.shape[0])\n   370→        region_ids[valid_channel] = channel_region_ids[cluster_channels[valid_channel]]\n   371→        cluster_regions = brain_regions.id2acronym(region_ids, mapping=\"Beryl\").astype(str)\n   372→\n   373→        spike_times_all.append(good_spike_times)\n   374→        spike_clusters_all.append(good_spike_clusters)\n   375→        cluster_regions_all.append(cluster_regions)\n   376→        cluster_offset += cluster_regions.shape[0]\n   377→\n   378→    if cluster_offset == 0:\n   379→        raise ValueError(f\"No good units remained for {spec.eid}\")\n   380→\n   381→    merged_spike_times = np.concatenate(spike_times_all)\n   382→    merged_spike_clusters = np.concatenate(spike_clusters_all)\n   383→    order = np.argsort(merged_spike_times)\n   384→    merged_spike_times = merged_spike_times[order]\n   385→    merged_spike_clusters = merged_spike_clusters[order]\n   386→    merged_cluster_regions = np.concatenate(cluster_regions_all)\n   387→    return merged_spike_times, merged_spike_clusters, merged_cluster_regions\n   388→\n   389→\n   390→def bin_spikes_by_trial(\n   391→    spike_times: np.ndarray,\n   392→    spike_clusters: np.ndarray,\n   393→    align_times: np.ndarray,\n   394→    n_units: int,\n   395→    binsize: float = BINSIZE,\n   396→    time_window: tuple[float, float] = TIME_WINDOW,\n   397→) -> list[np.ndarray]:\n   398→    interval_begs = align_times + time_window[0]\n   399→    interval_ends = align_times + time_window[1]\n   400→    n_bins = int(np.ceil((time_window[1] - time_window[0]) / binsize))\n   401→    start_idx = np.searchsorted(spike_times, interval_begs, side=\"left\")\n   402→    end_idx = np.searchsorted(spike_times, interval_ends, side=\"left\")\n   403→\n   404→    out: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   405→    for i in range(len(align_times)):\n   406→        i0 = start_idx[i]\n   407→        i1 = end_idx[i]\n   408→        if i1 <= i0:\n   409→            out.append(np.zeros((n_units, n_bins), dtype=np.float16))\n   410→            continue\n   411→\n   412→        rel = spike_times[i0:i1] - interval_begs[i]\n   413→        bin_idx = np.floor(rel / binsize).astype(np.int64)\n   414→        valid = (bin_idx >= 0) & (bin_idx < n_bins)\n   415→        if not np.any(valid):\n   416→            out.append(np.zeros((n_units, n_bins), dtype=np.float16))\n   417→            continue\n   418→\n   419→        flat = spike_clusters[i0:i1][valid] * n_bins + bin_idx[valid]\n   420→        counts = np.bincount(flat, minlength=n_units * n_bins).reshape(n_units, n_bins)\n   421→        out.append(counts.astype(np.float16))\n   422→    return out\n   423→\n   424→\n   425→def map_choice(raw_choice: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n   426→    mapped = np.empty(raw_choice.shape[0], dtype=np.int8)\n   427→    mapped[raw_choice == 1] = 0\n   428→    mapped[raw_choice == -1] = 1\n   429→    if not np.all(np.isin(raw_choice, [-1, 1])):\n   430→        raise ValueError(\"Unexpected choice values encountered after filtering.\")\n   431→    return mapped\n   432→\n   433→\n   434→def map_prior(raw_prior: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n   435→    out = np.empty(raw_prior.shape[0], dtype=np.int8)\n   436→    mapper = {0.2: 0, 0.5: 1, 0.8: 2}\n   437→    for i, val in enumerate(raw_prior):\n   438→        key = round(float(val), 1)\n   439→        if key not in mapper:\n   440→            raise ValueError(f\"Unexpected probabilityLeft value {val}\")\n   441→        out[i] = mapper[key]\n   442→    return out\n   443→\n   444→\n   445→def build_session_payload(\n   446→    prepared: PreparedSession,\n   447→    wheel_edges: tuple[float, float],\n   448→    whisker_edges: tuple[float, float],\n   449→) -> tuple[str, list[np.ndarray], list[np.ndarray], list[np.ndarray], np.ndarray, float]:\n   450→    session_start = time.time()\n   451→    brain_regions = BrainRegions()\n   452→    kept_align_times = prepared.trials.loc[prepared.keep_mask, ALIGN_EVENT].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)\n   453→    spike_times, spike_clusters, cluster_regions = load_good_spikes_and_regions(prepared.spec, brain_regions)\n   454→    neural_trials = bin_spikes_by_trial(\n   455→        spike_times=spike_times,\n   456→        spike_clusters=spike_clusters,\n   457→        align_times=kept_align_times,\n   458→        n_units=cluster_regions.shape[0],\n   459→    )\n   460→\n   461→    choice = map_choice(prepared.choice_raw)\n   462→    prior = map_prior(prepared.prior_raw)\n   463→    wheel_bins = digitize_three_bins(prepared.wheel_cont, wheel_edges)\n   464→    whisker_bins = digitize_three_bins(prepared.whisker_cont, whisker_edges)\n   465→\n   466→    session_input: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   467→    session_output: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   468→    for trial_idx in range(len(neural_trials)):\n   469→        inp = np.vstack(\n   470→            [\n   471→                COMMON_RELATIVE_TIMES,\n   472→                np.full(NBINS, prepared.trial_number_in_block[trial_idx], dtype=np.float32),\n   473→            ]\n   474→        ).astype(np.float32)\n   475→        out = np.vstack(\n   476→            [\n   477→                np.full(NBINS, choice[trial_idx], dtype=np.int8),\n   478→                np.full(NBINS, prior[trial_idx], dtype=np.int8),\n   479→                wheel_bins[trial_idx],\n   480→                whisker_bins[trial_idx],\n   481→            ]\n   482→        ).astype(np.int8)\n   483→        session_input.append(inp)\n   484→        session_output.append(out)\n   485→\n   486→    elapsed = time.time() - session_start\n   487→    return prepared.spec.eid, neural_trials, session_input, session_output, cluster_regions.astype(str), elapsed\n   488→\n   489→\n   490→def make_processing_plot(\n   491→    prepared: PreparedSession,\n   492→    neural_trials: list[np.ndarray],\n   493→    wheel_edges: tuple[float, float],\n   494→ plt.close(fig)\n   549→\n   550→\n   551→def build_dataset(\n   552→    prepared_sessions: list[PreparedSession],\n   553→    wheel_edges: tuple[float, float],\n   554→    whisker_edges: tuple[float, float],\n   555→    show_processing: bool,\n   556→    n_workers: int,\n   557→) -> dict[str, Any]:\n   558→    neural_all: list[list[np.ndarray]] = []\n   559→    input_all: list[list[np.ndarray]] = []\n   560→    output_all: list[list[np.ndarray]] = []\n   561→    subject_names = sorted({ps.spec.subject for ps in prepared_sessions})\n   562→    subject_to_idx = {subject: i for i, subject in enumerate(subject_names)}\n   563→    subject_idx_list: list[int] = []\n   564→\n   565→    all_region_names: set[str] = set()\n   566→    session_region_names: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   567→    session_ids: list[str] = []\n   568→    excluded_session_notes: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n   569→\n   570→    t0 = time.time()\n   571→    if n_workers == 1:\n   572→        for session_i, prepared in enumerate(prepared_sessions, start=1):\n   573→            eid, neural_trials, session_input, session_output, cluster_regions, elapsed = build_session_payload(\n   574→                prepared, wheel_edges, whisker_edges\n   575→            )\n   576→            neural_all.append(neural_trials)\n   577→            input_all.append(session_input)\n   578→            output_all.append(session_output)\n   579→            session_region_names.append(cluster_regions)\n   580→            all_region_names.update(cluster_regions.tolist())\n   581→            session_ids.append(eid)\n   582→            subject_idx_list.append(subject_to_idx[prepared.spec.subject])\n   583→\n   584→            print(\n   585→                f\"[build] {session_i:03d}/{len(prepared_sessions)} {eid} \"\n   586→                f\"trials={len(neural_trials)} neurons={cluster_regions.shape[0]} \"\n   587→                f\"time={elapsed:.2f}s\"\n   588→            )\n   589→\n   590→            if show_processing and session_i <= 2:\n   591→                make_processing_plot(prepared, neural_trials, wheel_edges, whisker_edges)\n   592→    else:\n   593→        with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=n_workers) as pool:\n   594→            future_to_index = {\n   595→                pool.submit(build_session_payload, prepared, wheel_edges, whisker_edges): idx\n   596→                for idx, prepared in enumerate(prepared_sessions)\n   597→            }\n   598→            for completed_i, future in enumerate(as_completed(future_to_index), start=1):\n   599→                idx = future_to_index[future]\n   600→                prepared = prepared_sessions[idx]\n   601→                eid, neural_trials, session_input, session_output, cluster_regions, elapsed = future.result()\n   602→\n   603→                neural_all.append(neural_trials)\n   604→                input_all.append(session_input)\n   605→                output_all.append(session_output)\n   606→                session_region_names.append(cluster_regions)\n   607→                all_region_names.update(cluster_regions.tolist())\n   608→                session_ids.append(eid)\n   609→                subject_idx_list.append(subject_to_idx[prepared.spec.subject])\n   610→\n   611→                print(\n   612→                    f\"[build] {completed_i:03d}/{len(prepared_sessions)} {eid} \"\n   613→                    f\"trials={len(neural_trials)} neurons={cluster_regions.shape[0]} \"\n   614→                    f\"time={elapsed:.2f}s\"\n   615→                )\n   616→\n   617→    brain_region_list = sorted(all_region_names)\n   618→    brain_region_to_idx = {name: i for i, name in enumerate(brain_region_list)}\n   619→    brain_region_idx = [\n   620→        np.array([brain_region_to_idx[name] for name in session_regions], dtype=np.int64)\n   621→        for session_regions in session_region_names\n   622→    ]\n   623→\n   624→    print(f\"[build] completed in {time.time() - t0:.2f}s\")\n   625→\n   626→    data = {\n   627→        \"neural\": neural_all,\n   628→        \"input\": input_all,\n   629→        \"output\": output_all,\n   630→        \"subjects\": subject_names,\n   631→        \"subject_idx\": Data Loading\n\n### `prepare_data` — `ibl_data_utils.py`\n**Purpose:** Top-level session loader. Merges all probes within a session, loads spikes, trials, and behavior streams, and packages everything into a single metadata-rich structure.\n\n### `load_spiking_data` — `ibl_data_utils.py`\n**Key parameters:** `pid` (probe insertion ID), `qc` (optional QC filter)\n**What it does:** Loads spike sorting outputs (`spikes.times`, `spikes.clusters`) and cluster metadata via `SpikeSortingLoader`. When `qc=None` (the reference caching default), ALL clusters are loaded without filtering. When `qc` is passed, it applies the label threshold.\n**Important detail:** Reference caching always calls with `qc=None`. The QC flag `good_clusters = (label >= 1)` is still recorded in metadata but is NOT used to filter spikes during caching.\n\n### `load_target_behavior` — `ibl_data_utils.py`\n**What it does:** Loads continuous behavior streams — specifically wheel speed and whisker motion energy.\n- **Wheel speed:** Loads `_ibl_wheel.timestamps.npy` + `_ibl_wheel.position.npy`, interpolates position to 1000 Hz via `brainbox.behavior.wheel.interpolate_position`, computes velocity with `velocity_filtered` (corner frequency 20 Hz, order 8 Butterworth), then takes `abs(velocity)`.\n- **Whisker motion energy:** Prefers `leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy` + `_ibl_leftCamera.times.npy`; falls back to right camera if left is absent. If camera timestamps are longer than the motion-energy array, timestamps are trimmed from the front (i.e., `timestamps = timestamps[-len(data):]`).\n\nReimplemented verbatim in `convert_data.py`:\n\n```python\ndef load_wheel_speed(session_path: Path) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    alf_path = session_path / \"alf\"\n    timestamps = np.load(resolve_latest(alf_path, \"**/_ibl_wheel.timestamps.npy\"))\n    position  = np.load(resolve_latest(alf_path, \"**/_ibl_wheel.position.npy\"))\n    interp_pos, interp_times = interpolate_position(timestamps, position, freq=1000)\n    velocity, _ = velocity_filtered(interp_pos, fs=1000, corner_frequency=20, order=8)\n    return interp_times.astype(np.float64), np.abs(velocity).astype(np.float32)\n\ndef load_whisker_motion_energy(session_path: Path) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, str]:\n    alf_path = session_path / \"alf\"\n    for view in (\"left\", \"right\"):\n        me_candidates = sorted(alf_path.glob(f\"**/{view}Camera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy\"))\n        ts_candidates = sorted(alf_path.glob(f\"**/_ibl_{view}Camera.times.npy\"))\n        if not me_candidates or not ts_candidates:\n            continue\n        motion_energy = np.load(me_candidates[-1])\n        timestamps    = np.load(ts_candidates[-1])\n        if timestamps.shape[0] > motion_energy.shape[0]:\n            timestamps = timestamps[-motion_energy.shape[0]:]   # front-trim repair\n        return timestamps.astype(np.float64), motion_energy.astype(np.float32), view\n    raise FileNotFoundError(...)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 2. Neuron QC (Filtering)\n\n### `load_spiking_data` with `qc=None`\n**Rule during caching:** `qc=None` — no cluster is dropped at load time.\n**Rule recorded in metadata:** `good_clusters = (clusters.metrics.label >= 1)`.\n**Rule applied in conversion (and in final analyses):** `label >= 1`. Across the 699 frozen-release probe insertions this yields exactly **75,708 well-isolated neurons**, matching the data paper exactly.\n\n```python\n# Reference rule (from load_good_spikes_and_regions in convert_data.py):\nmetrics = pd.read_parquet(metrics_path, columns=[\"label\"])\ngood_rows = metrics[\"label\"].to_numpy(copy=False) >= 1\n```\n\n---\n\n## 3. Trial Filtering\n\n### `load_trials_and_mask` — `ibl_data_utils.py`\n**What it does:** Builds a boolean inclusion mask for each trial. The exact criteria:\n\n| Criterion | Threshold |\n|---|---|\n| `stimOn_times` not null | required |\n| `choice` not null | required |\n| `feedback_times` not null | required |\n| `probabilityLeft` not null | required |\n| `firstMovement_times` not null | required |\n| `feedbackType` not null | required |\n| Reaction time (`firstMovement_times - stimOn_times`) | 0.08 s ≤ RT ≤ 2.0 s |\n| Trial duration (`feedback_times - goCue_times`) | ≤ 10.0 s |\n| `choice != 0` | exclude no-choice trials |\n\nReimplemented verbatim:\n\n```python\ndef compute_trial_mask(trials: pd.DataFrame) -> np.ndarray:\n    rt = trials[\"firstMovement_times\"] - trials[\"stimOn_times\"]\n    mask = (\n        ~trials[\"stimOn_times\"].isnull()\n        & ~trials[\"choice\"].isnull()\n        & ~trials[\"feedback_times\"].isnull()\n        & ~trials[\"probabilityLeft\"].isnull()\n        & ~trials[\"firstMovement_times\"].isnull()\n        & ~trials[\"feedbackType\"].isnull()\n        & (rt >= 0.08)\n        & (rt <= 2.0)\n        & ((trials[\"feedback_times\"] - trials[\"goCue_times\"]) <= 10.0)\n        & (trials[\"choice\"] != 0)\n    )\n    return mask.to_numpy(dtype=bool)\n```\n\nAn additional behavioral-coverage mask is then ANDed: trials where the wheel or whisker interpolation window fails (no data within one `binsize` of each interval edge) are also excluded.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Spike Binning\n\n### `bin_spiking_data` / `get_spike_data_per_interval` — `ibl_data_utils.py`\n**Alignment event:** `stimOn_times`\n**Time window:** `(-0.5, 1.5)` seconds\n**Bin size:** `0.02` s (20 ms)\n**Number of bins:** `ceil(2.0 / 0.02) = 100`\n**Output shape per trial:** `(n_neurons, 100)` spike counts\n\nThe binning uses `np.searchsorted` on the merged spike-time array to find each trial's interval, then `np.floor(relative_time / binsize)` for bin assignment, and `np.bincount` on the flattened `cluster * n_bins + bin` index:\n\n```python\ndef bin_spikes_by_trial(spike_times, spike_clusters, align_times, n_units,\n                        binsize=0.02, time_window=(-0.5, 1.5)):\n    interval_begs = align_times + time_window[0]\n    interval_ends = align_times + time_window[1]\n    n_bins = int(np.ceil((time_window[1] - time_window[0]) / binsize))  # 100\n    start_idx = np.searchsorted(spike_times, interval_begs, side=\"left\")\n    end_idx   = np.searchsorted(spike_times, interval_ends, side=\"left\")\n    out = []\n    for i in range(len(align_times)):\n        rel     = spike_times[start_idx[i]:end_idx[i]] - interval_begs[i]\n        bin_idx = np.floor(rel / binsize).astype(np.int64)\n        valid   = (bin_idx >= 0) & (bin_idx < n_bins)\n        flat    = spike_clusters[...][valid] * n_bins + bin_idx[valid]\n        counts  = np.bincount(flat, minlength=n_units * n_bins).reshape(n_units, n_bins)\n        out.append(counts.astype(np.float16))\n    return out\n```\n\n---\n\n## 5. Behavior Processing\n\n### `get_behavior_per_interval` — `ibl_data_utils.py`\n**What it does:** For each trial, extracts the behavior signal within `[stimOn + window[0], stimOn + window[1]]`, checks that the raw samples cover the interval to within one `binsize` at each edge, then linearly interpolates onto `n_bins` evenly-spaced sample points.\n\n**Interpolation grid:** `np.linspace(interval_beg + binsize, interval_end, n_bins)`\nThis is `linspace(-0.5 + 0.02, 1.5, 100)` = sample points at `-0.48, -0.46, ..., 1.50` s relative to stimulus onset.\n\n**Coverage check:** A trial is included only if:\n- at least one raw sample exists in the window\n- no NaNs in the raw samples\n- `|interval_beg - first_sample_time| <= binsize`\n- `|interval_end - last_sample_time| <= binsize`\n\n```python\ndef interpolate_behavior_per_trial(target_times, target_vals, align_times,\n                                   binsize=0.02, time_window=(-0.5, 1.5)):\n    interval_begs = align_times + time_window[0]\n    interval_ends = align_times + time_window[1]\n    n_bins = int(np.ceil((time_window[1] - time_window[0]) / binsize))\n    ...\n    for i in range(len(align_times)):\n        ...\n        if abs(t_beg - curr_times[0]) > binsize: continue\n        if abs(t_end - curr_times[-1]) > binsize: continue\n        x_interp = np.linspace(t_beg + binsize, t_end, n_bins)\n        y_interp = np.interp(x_interp, curr_times, curr_vals)\n        outputs[i] = y_interp.astype(np.float32)\n        mask[i] = True\n    return outputs, mask\n```\n\n### Wheel speed processing specifically\n1. Load raw `_ibl_wheel.timestamps.npy` + `_ibl_wheel.position.npy`\n2. `interpolate_position(timestamps, position, freq=1000)` → uniform 1000 Hz position trace\n3. `velocity_filtered(interp_pos, fs=1000, corner_frequency=20, order=8)` → low-pass filtered velocity\n4. `np.abs(velocity)` → speed (always non-negative)\n5. Align/interpolate per trial using `interpolate_behavior_per_trial`\n\n### Whisker motion energy processing specifically\n1. Prefer `leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy`; fall back to `rightCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy`\n2. Front-trim camera timestamps if longer than motion energy array\n3. Align/interpolate per trial using `interpolate_behavior_per_trial`\n\n---\n\n## 6. Temporal Alignment\n\n**Alignment event:** `stimOn_times` (stimulus onset)\n**Time window:** `(-0.5, 1.5)` seconds relative to `stimOn_times`\n**Interval length:** 2.0 s\n**Bin size:** 0.02 s → 100 bins per trial\n**Common timepoint grid:** `np.linspace(-0.5 + 0.02, 1.5, 100)` = `[-0.48, -0.46, ..., 1.50]` s\n\nThe same grid applies to both neural data and all behavior streams. The reference papers use first-movement onset for dynamic behaviors and a different window for prior, but the implementation uses a single stimulus-onset-aligned grid for all variables.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Choice, Prior/Block, Wheel Speed, Whisker Motion Energy Computation\n\n### Choice — `bin_behaviors` in `ibl_data_utils.py`\n**Source:** `trials.choice` column\n**Mapping:**\n- `choice == 1` (moved left) → `0` (\"left\")\n- `choice == -1` (moved right) → `1` (\"right\")\n- `choice == 0` trials are excluded by the trial mask\n\n```python\ndef map_choice(raw_choice: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    mapped = np.empty(raw_choice.shape[0], dtype=np.int8)\n    mapped[raw_choice == 1]  = 0   # left\n    mapped[raw_choice == -1] = 1   # right\n    return mapped\n```\n\n### Prior / Block — `bin_behaviors` in `ibl_data_utils.py`\n**Source:** `trials.probabilityLeft` column\n**Values in data:** `0.2`, `0.5`, `0.8`\n**Mapping:**\n- `0.2` → `0`  (right block)\n- `0.5` → `1`  (unbiased / first 90 trials)\n- `0.8` → `2`  (left block)\n- Value is a **scalar per trial** (not time-varying); repeated across all 100 bins to fill the output matrix shape `(4, 100)`.\n\n```python\ndef map_prior(raw_prior: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    out = np.empty(raw_prior.shape[0], dtype=np.int8)\n    mapper = {0.2: 0, 0.5: 1, 0.8: 2}\n    for i, val in enumerate(raw_prior):\n        out[i] = mapper[round(float(val), 1)]\n    return out\n```\n\n**Trial number in block** (input feature derived from prior): counts position within the current `probabilityLeft` block, computed on the full unfiltered trial table before masking, then subsetted:\n\n```python\ndef compute_trial_number_in_block(prob_left: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    out = np.zeros(len(prob_left), dtype=np.int16)\n    prev, counter = None, 0\n    for i, val in enumerate(prob_left):\n        current = None if pd.isna(val) else float(val)\n        counter = 1 if (i == 0 or current != prev) else counter + 1\n        out[i] = counter\n        prev = current\n    return out\n```\n\n### Wheel Speed Discretization\nContinuous interpolated speed traces are discretized using **global tertile edges** computed across all kept time-points in all included sessions:\n\n```python\n# Compute global edges (pass 1 aggregate):\nall_wheel = np.concatenate([ps.wheel_cont.reshape(-1) for ps in prepared_sessions])\nq1, q2 = np.quantile(all_wheel, [1/3, 2/3])   # → wheel_edges\n\n# Apply per time-bin per trial:\ndef digitize_three_bins(values, edges):\n    low, high = edges\n    return np.digitize(values, bins=np.array([low, high]), right=False).astype(np.int8)\n    # result: 0 = low, 1 = medium, 2 = high\n```\n\nCategories: `0` = low, `1` = medium, `2` = high. Each of the 100 bins per trial gets its own category value, making it a time-varying output.\n\n### Whisker Motion Energy Discretization\nSame approach as wheel speed: global tertile edges over all kept time-points across all sessions, applied per bin:\n\n```python\nall_whisker = np.concatenate([ps.whisker_cont.reshape(-1) for ps in prepared_sessions])\nq1, q2 = np.quantile(all_whisker, [1/3, 2/3])   # → whisker_edges\n# digitize_three_bins(...) → 0/1/2 per time bin\n```\n\nBy construction, the global tertile binning yields exactly uniform distributions: 33.33% low, 33.33% medium, 33.33% high.\n\n---\n\n## Summary Table\n\n| Aspect | Value |\n|---|---|\n| Alignment event | `stimOn_times` |\n| Time window | `(-0.5, 1.5)` s |\n| Bin size | `0.02` s (20 ms) |\n| Number of bins | 100 |\n| Timepoint grid | `linspace(-0.48, 1.50, 100)` s |\n| Neuron QC | `clusters.metrics.label >= 1` (during caching: `qc=None`, all loaded) |\n| Trial filter: RT | 0.08 s ≤ `firstMovement_times - stimOn_times` ≤ 2.0 s |\n| Trial filter: duration | `feedback_times - goCue_times` ≤ 10.0 s |\n| Trial filter: no-choice | `choice != 0` excluded |\n| Trial filter: nulls | 6 columns must be non-null |\n| Wheel speed | `abs(velocity_filtered(interpolate_position(..., freq=1000), corner_freq=20Hz, order=8))` |\n| Whisker ME source | left camera preferred;
